Observation/ Recommendation 
 
4.13.4 The Committee notes the significant budgetary allocations for CPOs in the upcoming 
fiscal year, with a total budget of ₹1,754.88 crores for 2025-26. It observes that the budget 
includes both revenue and capital components, with a notable increase in allocations to support 
modernization and operational enhancements across various agencies. The Committee urges 
the Ministry to ensure that these allocations are utilized efficiently, particularly in areas like 
narcotics control and cybercrime, where technological advancements are crucial. For instance, 
the Narcotics Control Bureau (NCB) has been allocated ₹193.77 crores for 2025-26, which 
should be utilized effectively to enhance drug detection capabilities and operationalize new 
field units. Similarly, the Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre (I4C) has been allocated 
₹142.81 crores, which should focus on expanding cyber awareness programs and establishing 
specialized units like the Cyber Commandos.The Committee notes that the drug menace is 
affecting the young population of our country in a bad way.  The Ministry of Home Affairs 
should take more steps to address the issue in association with NCB. 
 
4.13.5 The Committee recommends the NCRB to effectively utilize its increased budget for 
2025-26, to enhance its data analysis capabilities. It emphasizes the importance of leveraging 
advanced technologies, such as artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning, to improve the 
depth and accuracy of crime statistics. The Committee urges NCRB to ensure that all crime 
data is published regularly and made accessible to law enforcement agencies and public. It also 
notes the publication of agitation data by the NCRB and suggests continuing of this 
publication. The Committee further recommends that NCRB to collaborate with international 
agencies, to adopt best practices in crime data collection and analysis.Additionally, it suggests 
that the Directorate of Coordination and Police Wireless (DCPW) mayfocus on upgrading 
communication systems to ensure seamless coordination among law enforcement agencies. The 
Committee emphasizes that each CPO should provide regular updates on expenditure and 
project implementation to ensure that the allocated funds are used effectively to enhance 
public safety and security. The Committee notes that cybercrime in the country has increased 
manifold. A notable characteristic of cybercrimes is that they often involve multiple State and 
police jurisdictions.Therefore, it is essential that the Police authorities must get the power of 
seamless jurisdiction to deal with the Cyber Crimes.  The Committee recommends that a 
separate Central Police Force may be established on the lines of NIA or CBI to deal with Cyber 
Crimes. 
 
4.14 FORENSIC SCIENCES FACILITIES AND INFRASTRUCTURE 
 
4.14.1 Under the Scheme, the MHA informed that there are seven Central Forensic Science 
Laboratories (CFSLs) under the Directorate of Forensic Science Services (DFSS), which include 
DNA analysis facilities. Additionally, there are 32 State Forensic Science Laboratories (FSLs) and 
97 Regional FSLs across States and Union Territories, as per the Bureau of Police Research 
&Development's (BPR&D)andData on Police Organizations (DoPO) Report 2023. Under the 
Nirbhaya-funded scheme, DNA analysis facilities in 30 State FSLs and cyber forensic facilities in 13 
State FSLs have been enhanced. This initiative aims to strengthen forensic capabilities in States to 
improve crime investigation and prosecution. The funds allocated under the head Criminology and 
Forensic Science are given in the table below:

4.14.3 The National Forensic Science University (NFSU) has been allocated ₹80 crores in both the 
Budget Estimate (BE) and Revised Estimate (RE) for 2024-25, with an increased allocation of ₹120 
crores for 2025-26. Established as an Institute of National Importance, NFSU aims to advance 
education, research, training and skilling in policing, criminology and forensic sciences. The 
additional funds allocated for 2025-26 are intended to support NFSU's research projects, consultancy 
services and training programs for law enforcement agencies and allied organizations in forensic 
sciences. This expansion will enable NFSU to expedite its activities in line with its mandate.

45 
 
 
4.14.5    Variation between BE 2025-26 and BE 2024-25: With the enactment of the New Criminal 
Laws which mandates forensic investigation for offences involving punishment of 7 years or more, a 
significant increase in the workload of forensic science laboratories is expected. The increased 
allocation is towards the capacity building and to cater for the less release in the FY 2024-25. 
Observation/ Recommendation 
4.14.6 The Committee notes the significant efforts to enhance forensic capabilities, 
particularly through the strengthening of DNA and cyber forensic facilities under the 
Nirbhaya Fund. It recommends the Ministry to continue to invest in modernizing forensic 
infrastructure, focusing on advanced technologies to improve analysis efficiency. The 
Committee suggests the Ministry to ensure that all forensic laboratories, both central and 
State, are equipped with the latest equipment and trained personnel to handle complex cases 
effectively. It emphasizes the importance of maintaining a centralized database of forensic data 
to aid in investigations and policy-making. Additionally, it suggests that the Ministry to 
consider establishing more regional forensic laboratories to increase accessibility and reduce 
the burden on existing facilities. The Committee notes that new Criminal Laws mandates 
forensic investigation for offences involving punishment of 7 years or more.  The Committee 
also notes that there are very few forensic science laboratories.  The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that every district of the country must have a forensic laboratory to deal with the 
criminal investigation. The Committee is aware that criminal investigation is a State subject 
but given the financial health of the States, the Union Government should financially support 
the establishment of forensic laboratories in each district of the country. 
4.14.7 The Committee also recommends that the Ministry needs to prioritize the establishment 
of off-campuses of the National Forensic Sciences University (NFSU) and Centers of Excellence 
to provide quality training and education in forensic sciences. It suggests that mobile forensic 
vans be deployed to enhance on-site forensic capabilities, particularly in remote areas.

Observation/Recommendation  
4.15.6 The Committee notes the significant efforts of the Ministry to enhance border 
infrastructure across various international borders. The Committee recommends that the 
Government needs to expedite the completion of ongoing fencing projects, ensuring that all 
allocated funds are utilized efficiently. It also recommends conducting regular assessments to 
identify gaps in border infrastructure and ensure that allocations are aligned with strategic 
priorities. The Committee also wishes the Ministry to prepare the data of influx of immigrants 
like Bangladeshis, Rohingyas and from other countries. The Committee notes that Rohingyas 
are entering and settling illegally in various parts of the country. The Committee recommends 
that the Ministry of Home Affairs should take effective steps to identify the illegally settled 
Rohingyas  and repatriate them to their parent countries. 
4.15.7 The Committee suggests that the Ministry needs to consider establishing more 
integrated check posts to facilitate smoother trade and travel while maintaining security 
standards. The Committee recommends that the MHA needs to continue to enhance border 
security through the expansion of smart fencing projects, which are part of the Comprehensive 
Integrated Border Management System (CIBMS). These initiatives should use advanced 
technologies such as thermal imagers, fiber-optic sensors, radar and sonar systems to create an 
invisible electronic barrier that aids in real-time surveillance and intrusion detection, thereby 
strengthening India's border security. 
4.16 SCHEMES FOR THE SAFETY OF WOMEN 
4.16.1 The budget allocation under this head pertains to expenditures from the Nirbhaya Fund in the 
Public Account, specifically for schemes focused on enhancing women's safety. The funds are 
administered by the Ministry through releases to State / UT Governments for certain identified 
projects such as Safe City Projects, Strengthening of Forensic Science Labs, Emergency Response 
Support System (ERSS) and Cyber Crime Prevention against Women and Children (CCPWC). The 
proposed expenditure out of the Nirbhaya Fund seeks to utilize the unspent balance lying at credit in 
the Fund. In ERSS, a helpline number 112 provides for the deployment of an advanced solution to 
ensure an improved high-impact value-added single number-based emergency response system 
covering every area in the country. 

49 
 
4.16.2 In a reply to a query, the MHA informed the Committee that the budget allocation under this 
head is as follows:- 
Table 4.17 
 
Budget under Schemes for the Safety of Women 
 
(amount in crores of ₹) 
 Actuals 2023-24 BE 2024-25 RE 2024-25 BE 2025-26 
Revenue 375.85 1055.00 365.75 886.12 
Capital -- 50.00 9.00 74.00 
Total 375.85 1105.00 374.75 960.12 
 
4.16.3 The allocation of ₹180.12 crores in BE 2025-26 under the sub-head Emergency Response 
Support System (ERSS) will be utilized to upgraded the existing system with the ERSS Phase II in 
the remaining States/UTs, ensuring a standardized and efficient emergency response mechanism 
nationwide. ERSS Phase-II has already been successfully implemented in 14 States/UTs 
(Chandigarh, Mizoram, Puducherry, Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu, Odisha, Andaman 
and Nicobar Islands, Arunachal Pradesh, Goa, Himachal Pradesh, Lakshadweep, Andhra Pradesh, 
Karnataka, Punjab, and Meghalaya). The allocated funds will be utilized across key categories, 
including hardware infrastructure for system deployment, connectivity enhancements to ensure 
uninterrupted operations, and COTS software procurement for Emergency Response Centers 
(ERCs). Additionally, funds will be directed toward Facility Management Services (FMS) for 
maintenance, hiring technical resources and developers, and engaging C-DAC for project 
management and technical support. Furthermore, cloud services for disaster recovery (DR) across all 
States/UTs will be implemented to enhance data security and system resilience. 
4.16.4  The Ministry has informed the Committee that it is undertaking 12 projects for safety of 
women for which funds have been released to States/UTs i.e- Emergency Response Support System 
(ERSS), Central Victim Compensation Fund (CVCF), Cyber Crime Prevention against Women & 
Children (CCPWC), 3 Delhi Police projects, SafeCity Project in 8 cities, Strengthening DNA 
analysis, cyber forensic & related facilities in 23 SFSLs, Training of Investigation Officers/ 
Prosecution Officers/ Medical Officers in forensic evidence and procurement of Forensic Kits for 
sexual Assault cases, Setting-up and strengthening Anti-Human Trafficking Units (ATHUs) in all 
districts of States & UTs, Setting-up/ strengthening Women Help Desks in Police Stations in all 
States & UTs (covering 10,000 Police Stations). The following are the sub-schemes being 
implemented under the Nirbhaya Funded Scheme: 
 Strengthening of DNA Analysis and Cyber Forensic Capacities in State FSLs: This Ministry 
has so far approved projects of 30 States/UTs with a total cost of ₹245.29 crores, out of which 
funds to the tune of ₹189.54 crores have been released so far and ₹154.42 crores have been 
utilized till date as per PFMS. Through this assistance, DNA facilities in 30 State FSLs and 
Cyber facilities in 13 State FSLs have been strengthened as per their requests/proposals. 
 
 Training, Capacity Building and Awareness Programmes: Under the umbrella scheme 
"Safety of Women”, the Government is undertaking training for Investigating Officers, 
Prosecutors, and Medical Officers from States/UTs in collection, storage and handling of DNA 
evidence and use of Sexual Assault Evidence Collection Kits. So far 33,357 Investigating 
Officers, Prosecutors and Medical Officers have been trained by the Bureau of Police Research 
and Development (BPR&D) and the LNJN National Institute of Criminology and Forensic 
Sciences (now Delhi campus of the National Forensic Sciences University). The Ministry of 

50 
 
Home Affairs has also distributed 18020 Sexual Assault Evidence Collection Kits to the 
States/UTs as part of this training. Out of the total approved outlay of ₹76.5 crores under the 
scheme, so far, ₹52.24 crores have been utilized. 
 
Observation/ Recommendation 
 
4.16.5 The Committee acknowledges and commends the Ministry's substantial efforts to 
enhance women's safety through various schemes under the Nirbhaya Fund. Initiatives such as 
the Emergency Response Support System (ERSS), Safe City Projects and the strengthening of 
Forensic Science Labs reflect a holistic approach to combat violence against women. The 
Committee observes that the allocation for ERSS Phase II in 2025-26 will focus on upgrading 
the existing system to ensure a standardized and efficient emergency response mechanism 
nationwide.  However, the Committee notes the underutilization of funds allocated under these 
schemes. The Committee recommends the Ministry to develop a centralized digital dashboard 
to track real-time progress of projects under the Nirbhaya Fund.  Further, a mid-term review 
mechanism should be institutionalized to identify underperforming projects and reallocate 
unspent funds to high-impact initiatives. 
 
4.16.6  The Committee recommends that the Ministry needs to expand training programs for 
investigating officers, prosecutors and medical officers to improve the handling of forensic 
evidence and sexual assault cases. It emphasizes the need for increased awareness and 
capacity-building initiatives to address violence against women effectively. The Committee also 
suggests the MHA to consider integrating more technology-driven solutions, such as AI-
powered surveillance systems and mobile apps, to enhance women's safety in public spaces.  
 
4.17 INTER-OPERABLE CRIMINAL JUSTICE  SYSTEM  (ICJS) 
 
4.17.1 The Provision is for a new project on Inter-Operable Criminal Justice System (ICJS). The 
project involves enhancement in the criminal justice IT systems for a paperless operation, 
interlinking the IT systems for seamless data exchange and also to provide for new technologies for 
smart policing.

4.17.2 The Committee was informed by the MHA that the State Action Plan for hardware, network 
connectivity, capacity building and IEC activities under the ICJS 2.0 project has been approved in 
accordance with ICJS guidelines for all States and Union Territories. The allocated funds for      
2025-26 will be utilized to meet the procurement requirements of all States and Union Territories for 
these purposes. Further it has been informed that the payments for applications such as CCTNS, Cr 
PI, Mediation Layer and ICJS constitute a committed liability. 
 
4.17.3 The Ministry in its reply to the Committee has mentioned that the fund utilization under this 
head for 2023-24 was ₹66.59 crores and for 2024-25 (till December 31, 2024), it was ₹75.69 crores. 

51 
 
The Ministry has further informed the Committee regarding the implementation of new applications 
like eSakshya and Nyaya Shruti, the budget supports these initiatives by allocating funds for hosting 
them on a pay-per-use cloud infrastructure provided by the National Informatics Centre (NIC). This 
infrastructure will facilitate the efficient operation of these applications, which are designed to 
expedite investigations and judicial processes in compliance with new criminal laws. Other 
applications, such as e-Summons, will also benefit from this arrangement. 
Observation / Recommendation 
4.17.4 The Committee notes and appreciates the significant efforts by the Ministry to enhance 
the criminal justice system through the Inter-Operable Criminal Justice System (ICJS). This 
initiative has the potential to revolutionize the way data is shared and utilized across different 
pillars of the justice system. The Committee recommends that MHA needs to prioritize the 
effective implementation of ICJS across all States and Union territories by ensuring timely 
allocation and utilization of funds. It suggests that the Ministry of Home Affairs work closely 
with the National Crime Records Bureau (NCRB) and the National Informatics Centre (NIC) 
to ensure that the cloud-based infrastructure is secure and scalable. The Committee further 
recommends that necessary capacity building and training programs for officials involved in 
the ICJS needs to be given to ensure they are equipped to handle the advanced technologies 
and data management systems effectively.  
4.18 MODERNIZATION OF PRISONS  
 
4.18.1 The Ministry has been implementing the "Modernization of Prisons" project over five 
financial years, from 2021-22 to 2025-26. This project aims to enhance security infrastructure and 
logistical facilities in jails through technological interventions and the creation of high-security 
infrastructure. It also focuses on correctional administration through programs and initiatives 
centered on skilling, rehabilitation and behavior change. The Government prioritizes correctional 
administration and inmate rehabilitation to promote reintegration into society, with the goals of 
decriminalization and de-radicalization. This project modernizes prisons by enhancing security and 
introducing technology into daily administration to improve the safety and security of jails and 
inmates. 
 
4.18.2 According to the Ministry, this project encompasses several key components designed to 
enhance security and promote rehabilitation. These include upgrading video conferencing 
infrastructure, implementing body-worn cameras and developing motivational videos to encourage 
inmates to abandon criminal lifestyles. Jamming solutions and security poles will bolster security 
measures. Furthermore, the project emphasizes correctional programs such as counseling and 
therapies, facilitated by hiring instructors, teachers, behavioral experts, correctional administration 
experts and psychologists to support inmates. A significant aspect of the project involves 
establishing high-security prisons in States to securely house hard-core criminals and prevent them 
from influencing other prisoners. Initial funding has been released to these identified States for this 
purpose. The budget allocated under this head is shown in the table mentioned below:- 
Table No. 4.19 
                                                                                                                                            (amount in crores of ₹) 
 Actuals 2023-24 BE 2024-25 RE 2024-25 BE 2025-26 
Revenue 86.95 300.00 75.00 300.00 
 

52 
 
4.18.3 The above table shows that only revenue expenditure is allotted, with no allocation for capital 
expenditure. Revenue figures are ₹86.95 crores (actuals for 2023-24), ₹300.00 crores (BE for 2024-
25), ₹75.00 crores (RE for 2024-25), and ₹300.00 crores (BE for 2025-26), indicating a sharp drop in 
the revised estimates for 2024-25 before returning to the original projection in 2025-26. In a reply to 
the Committee, the Ministry explained that the allocated funds for this year will be used to address a 
funding gap created by a shift from CNA Model 2 to CNA TSA Hybrid Model 1A. While ₹285 
crores was previously allocated for prison modernization, the model change resulted in unspent 
funds being returned. The Ministry stated that with States now commencing high-security prison 
construction after overcoming administrative delays, substantial funding is required in FY 2025-26 
to ensure effective scheme implementation. 
4.18.4 The Committee notes that as of 2022 over 75% of prisoners in India were undertrials which 
mean that their cases are pending for a long time.  In many of these cases it has been seen that the 
undertrial prisoners have been acquitted by Courts after remaining in jail for a long time. The Rules 
and Regulations applicable to the undertrials are more or less similar tothat of the hardened 
convicted criminals.  The jails are becoming a nursery and training centre for committing heinous 
crimes due to the intermingling of the hardened criminals with undertrials, who are in jail for less 
heinous crimes or petty offences.  This is mainly due to the fact that the undertrials and heinous 
criminals are housed in the same jail. The undertrials who are acquitted by the Court have to live 
with the stigma that they were in jails.  
4.18.5 In this regard, the Ministry has informed the Committee that the undertrials and convicted 
prisoners are to be kept separately in prisons as per the Model Prison Manual, 2016 and the Model 
Prisons & Correctional Services Act, which mandate the classification of prisoners into different 
categories, including convicted prisoners, undertrials, habitual offenders and high-risk prisoners. 
High-risk prisoners are to be housed in high-security prisons to prevent their negative influence on 
others. However, since prisons fall under the State List, the implementation of these provisions is the 
responsibility of State and UT Governments. To address the issue of undertrials, Section 479 of the 
Bharatiya Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita, 2023 (BNSS), allows for the release of undertrials who have 
served half of their maximum sentence or one-third in the case of first-time offenders. Prison 
superintendents are required to file bail applications for eligible undertrials. On Constitution Day, 
26th November 2024, MHA launched a Special Campaign to ensure the effective implementation of 
this provision, leading to the filing of 933 bail applications and the release of 336 eligible prisoners. 
States and UTs have been directed to continue this initiative and submit monthly reports to the 
Ministry. 
4.18.6 With respect to the usage of mobile phones in the jails, the Ministry informed the Committee 
that the MHA has issued guidelines to States and Union Territories (UTs) through the Model Prison 
Manual and the Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act. These guidelines emphasize the use of 
modern jamming solutions and search equipment to tackle the mobile phone usage within prisons. 
The Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act categorizes the use of mobile phones and other 
contraband as a "prison offense" prescribing punishments for such offenses. To support these efforts, 
the MHA is providing financial assistance to States/UTs under the Modernization Prisons Project. 
This funding is used for installing security equipment such as mobile phone jammers, baggage 
scanners and other search devices to prevent the use of contraband items like mobile phones inside 
prisons. 
 
 

53 
 
Observation / Recommendation 
4.18.7 The Committee appreciates the Ministry for its ongoing efforts in the modernization of 
prisons, which focus on enhancing security and promoting rehabilitation through technological 
advancements, infrastructure improvements and effective correctional administration. 
However, considering the funding gap caused by the transition to the CNA TSA Hybrid Model 
1A, the Committee emphasizes the need for a smooth transition and calls on the Ministry to 
proactively address challenges to ensure the timely disbursement of funds. 
4.18.8 The Committee appreciates the Ministry's adherence to the Model Prison Manual, 2016 
and the Model Prisons & Correctional Services Act in ensuring the separation of undertrials 
and convicted prisoners. It emphasizes the necessity for full implementation of those guidelines 
by States and UTs, even though the prison administration falls under the State List. The 
Ministry’s efforts to address undertrials’ detention through Section 479 of the Bharatiya 
Nagarik Suraksha Sanhita, 2023 (BNSS) and the Special Campaign launched on Constitution 
Day are acknowledged by the Committee. The Committee suggests continued monitoring and 
collaboration with States and UTs to ensure the timely release of eligible undertrials and the 
submission of monthly progress reports.  
 
4.18.9  The Committee recommends that there may be separate centers of detention for 
undertrials. It should not be referred to as jails,instead they may be called correction centers 
or detention centers. Jails should be used only to accommodate the hardened convicted 
criminals.  This may go a long way in preventing the undertrials becoming hardened criminals, 
providing more scope for reforming the undertrials and escaping the stigma of jailed 
prisoners. 
4.18.10  Further, the Committee recommends the allocation of dedicated funds for capital 
expenditure alongside revenue expenditure to facilitate substantial and long-term 
improvements in prison infrastructure. It also recognizes the significant role of skill 
development and rehabilitation programs for prisoners and encourages the Ministry to ensure 
these initiatives receive sufficient funding and are effectively implemented in all States and 
Union Territories. 
4.18.11 The Committee notes that the Ministry has issued guidelines to the State and UTs 
through the Model Prison Manual and the Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act. The 
Committee recommends that these guidelines be fully implemented across all jurisdictions to 
address mobile phone usage in prisons. It also suggests enhancing the penalties prescribed in 
the Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act for using contrabandin prisons.  
4.19 Support to Poor Prisoners 
4.19.1 The budget allocation for supporting poor prisoners involves providing financial assistance to 
those who cannot afford bail or penalties. The allocation for this purpose was ₹1.25 crores in 2023-
24, with a significant increase to ₹20 crores in Budget Estimate (BE) 2024-25. However, the Revised 
Estimate (RE) for 2024-25 was reduced to ₹1 crores and for 2025-26, the allocation is ₹5 crores.  
 
4.19.2 The Support to Poor Prisoners Scheme was formulated by the Ministry in response to a 
budget announcement, with a budgetary provision of ₹20 crores for FY 2024-25. These funds were 

54 
 
allocated to States and Union Territories for effective implementation of the scheme, aiming to 
provide relief to eligible prisoners. Despite regular reviews conducted by MHA through virtual 
meetings and other communication channels, States and Union Territories did not fully utilize the 
allocated funds. Some States/UTs reported that prisoners were not eligible for the scheme due to 
involvement in multiple cases. In the reply by the Ministry to the Committee, it was informed that as 
per the information provided by States/UTs, only ₹17.46 lakhs have been utilized by the States/UTs 
till date on implementation of the scheme and benefit of the scheme has been provided to 77 eligible 
prisoners. 
Observation / Recommendation 
 
4.19.3 The Committee notes and appreciates the Government's efforts to support poor 
prisoners through this scheme. It observes that the slow pace of fund utilization by States and 
Union Territories is a significant concern. By effectively implementing this scheme, the 
Committee believes that it will help reduce the overcrowding of prisons by facilitating the 
release of prisoners who are unable to afford bail or fines. This will also contribute to reducing 
the number of undertrial prisoners, thereby decongesting prisons and ensuring that justice is 
delivered more efficiently. The Committee recommends that the Ministry to review the 
implementation of this scheme to identify barriers to fund utilization and ensure that funds are 
effectively used to benefit the intended beneficiaries. It suggests revising the eligibility criteria 
to include prisoners involved in multiple cases, ensuring broader coverage of the scheme. 
 
4.20   Land Port Authority of India (LPAI) 
4.20.1 The Ministry has informed the Committee that LPAI had proposed BE 2025-26 as ₹679 
crores against the BE 2024-25 amounting to ₹330 crores keeping in view the various ongoing 
construction activities and new works for the proposed development of Integrated Check Posts. 
Against this proposal, an outlay of ₹ 341.00 crores has been allocated in BE 2025-26. The reason for 
increase in BE 2025-26 from BE 2024-25 is due to enhancement amounting to ₹10.00crores under 
GIA-General, which is to meet expenditure on Other administrative expenses, Office expenses, 
administrative expenditure, Operational Expenditure, development of operational infrastructure and 
security Gadgets. Moreover, the number of operational ICPs has also been increased from 12 to 15 
in FY 2024-25. It may be mentioned that LPAI had demanded allocation of ₹95.00crores under GIA-
General for BE 2025-26 against which only ₹50.00crores has been allocated as against ₹ 40 crores in 
BE 2024-25.

Observation / Recommendation 
 
4.20.2   The Committee emphasizes the need for enhanced infrastructure development at 
Integrated Check Posts (ICPs) to improve cross-border trade efficiency. It notes that the 
increase in operational ICPs from 12 to 15 in FY 2024-25 is a positive step but suggests that 
more focus should be placed on completing ongoing projects efficiently. The Committee 
highlights the importance of timely completion of land acquisition and pre-construction 
activities for new ICPs. It emphasizes the need for swift approval processes to expedite project 
initiation. The Committee recommends prioritizing the completion of construction work at 
existing projects, such as the Land Ports at Sunauli, Sutarkandi, and Bhithamore, to ensure 
operationalization and efficiency in cross-border trade. 
4.20.3  The Committee emphasizes the need for robust infrastructure at all ICPs to enhance the 
efficiency of customs clearance and trade facilitation processes. It suggests that LPAI should 
engage in more extensive stakeholder consultations to identify and address common challenges 
faced by Land Customs Stations (LCPs) and ICPs, such as limited storage facilities and 
insufficient amenities. The Committee recommends conducting regular assessments to evaluate 
the efficiency of clearance processes at ICPs and identify areas for improvement. 
 
4.21 MODERNIZATION OF POLICE FORCES (MoPF) 
 
4.21.1 The Scheme seeks to bring under its fold many smaller schemes primarily aimed at 
facilitating and assisting the various State Police Forces, both in terms of finances and infrastructure 
up-gradation, in their efforts in combating various kinds of criminal activities, counter-insurgency 
operations, etc. The budgetary allocation under this head has been given below:- 
 
Table No. 4.21 
Fund allocation under Modernization of Police Forces 
 
(amount in crores of ₹) 
 Actuals 2023-24 BE 2024-25 RE 2024-25 BE 2025-26 
Revenue 2658.59 3720.13 2623.74 4069.24 
 
4.21.2 The variation between the Revised Estimate (RE) for 2024-25 and the Budget Estimate (BE) 
for the same year, particularly in areas affected by Left Wing Extremism (LWE), is primarily 
attributed to technical challenges encountered during the implementation of the SNA/CNA model. 
These issues led to delays in releasing funds under the "Assistance to States for Modernization of 
Police" scheme, resulting in reduced allocations in the revised estimate. Additionally, there was a 
decrease in claims received from States under the Special Central Assistance (SCA) scheme for the 
most affected LWE districts. The increase in the Budget Estimate for 2025-26 compared to 2024-25 
is intended to meet additional funding requirements for States and Union Territories, as well as to 
compensate for the reduced releases in the previous fiscal year. 
4.21.3 The provision for the Umbrella Scheme ‘Modernization of Police Forces of States and UTs 
comprises allocations for the following sub-schemes/components: 
 
 Assistance to States for Modernization of Police; 

56 
 
 Assistance to States for Special Projects/Programmes for upgrading police infrastructure; 
 Assistance to Central Agencies for LWE Management; 
 Civic Action Plan (CAP); 
 Media Plan (Advertising and Publicity); 
 Special Central Assistance (SCA) to 35 worst affected LWE districts; 
 Security Related Expenditure (SRE) [LWE – Central and State Sectors]; 
 SRE (J&K): Relief and Rehabilitation; 
 SRE (J&K): Security Environment; 
 SRE (J&K): Police; 
 SRE (NE); 
 Special Infrastructure Scheme (SIS) along with construction of 250 fortified Police Stations in 
LWE affected States; and 
 Reimbursement to States for administration of Central Acts and Regulations 
 Modernization of Forensic Capacities- to develop operationally independent high quality 
forensic sciences facilities in states/Union Territories, for aiding scientific and timely 
investigation 
 
4.21.4 The Ministry has allocated ₹520.51 crores to the Assistance to States and UTs for 
Modernization of Police (ASUMP) scheme in the Budget Estimate for 2024-25. Regular review 
meetings are held to monitor the utilization of these funds, with States and Union territories 
committing to reduce their unspent balances to ensure efficient use of resources. The Crime and 
Criminal Tracking Network and Systems (CCTNS) project has been fully implemented, covering all 
police stations with a national database of over 33.56 crores police records. 
 
4.21.5 For Left Wing Extremism (LWE) areas, the Security Related Expenditure (SRE) and Special 
Infrastructure Scheme (SIS) have been allocated significant funds. The SRE scheme supports 
operational needs in 58 districts across 10 states. With respect to the head SRE and Special 
infrastructure scheme for LWE, an allocation of ₹2463.62 crores for RE 2024-25 and ₹3481.27 
crores for BE 2025-26. The SIS focuses on infrastructure development, including fortified police 
stations, with an allocation of ₹155 crores for 2024-25 and an extension until March 2026 with a 
yearly outlay of ₹174 crores. 
 
4.21.6 The Ministry informed the Committee that Left Wing Extremism (LWE) has seen a consistent 
decline in both incidents and geographical spread in 2024, affecting 38 districts across nine states, 
down from 126 districts in 2013. The most affected areas include 12 districts in five States, while 
nine districts in five States are considered Districts of Concern. From 2010 to 2024, there has been a 
significant reduction in violence and resultant deaths. To combat LWE, development initiatives have 
been implemented, such as completing 5182 km of roads under the Road Requirement Plan-I and 
building 9349 km in affected areas. Telecommunication efforts have operationalized 2343 mobile 
towers, with 400 upgraded to 4G, and installed 1079 more in Phase-II. Additionally, 1975 mobile 
towers have been installed in uncovered villages and 1223 for 4G saturation. Financial inclusion 
measures have opened 1007 bank branches, 937 ATMs and made 37,850 Banking Correspondences 
operational in 30 LWE-affected districts, along with opening 5731 post offices in 90 districts. 
Observation/ Recommendation 
 
4.21.7 The Committee notes the significant efforts by the MHA to modernize police forces 
across India through the "Modernization of Police Forces" scheme. The Committee 
recommends that the Government has to prioritize the effective utilization of funds under the 

57 
 
"Assistance to States for Modernization of Police" scheme. It suggests that States and Union 
territories be encouraged to reduce unspent balances by ensuring timely implementation of 
projects. The Committee feels that the Ministry has to enhance collaboration with State 
Governments to address procedural delays and inefficiencies in the implementation of the 
Modernization of Police Forces scheme. Additionally, it recommends that the Ministry may 
explore international best practices in police modernization to enhance effectiveness and 
improve public safety. 
 
4.21.8 The Committee appreciates the Government's efforts in substantial reduction in Left 
Wing Extremism activities and recommends continued support for these initiatives to further 
reduce LWE influence and improve security and development in affected areas. Further, the 
Committee suggests that the Government may consider enhancing security in LWE areas by 
improving the capabilities of Special Forces and local police.

CHAPTER - V 
 
DEMAND NO. 52 TO 59 - UNION TERRITORIES 
 
5.1 Introduction 
 
5.1.1 The Demands for Grants (Nos. 52-59) relate to the country's eight Union Territories (UTs). 
Of these, five relate to UTs without Legislature, namely Andaman & Nicobar Islands (Demand No. 
52), Chandigarh (Demand No.53), Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu (Demand No.54), 
Ladakh (Demand No.55) and Lakshadweep (Demand No. 56). The remaining three Grants relate to 
UTs with Legislatures; Transfers to Delhi (Demand No. 57), Transfers to Jammu & Kashmir 
(Demand No.58) and Transfers to Puducherry (Demand No.59). As per Article 280 of the 
Constitution of India, the Finance Commission makes recommendation for distribution of funds 
between the Union and the States. The needs of UTs are fulfilled in the Union Budget. The overall 
Budgetary provisions for the years 2024-25 and 2025-26 and expenditure upto 31.01.2025 
pertaining to all the UTs without / with legislature have been given in the following tables:-

Observation/ Recommendation 
 
5.1.3 The Committee notes, from the perusal of the overall budgetary provisions and 
expenditure provided by the Ministry that most of the Union Territories have performed well 
with regard to the utilization of funds against allocation made in RE 2024-25. Although the 
funds utilization in Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu and Lakshadweep are 
comparatively low, still they have been able to utilize more that 70% of the funds against 
allocation made in RE 2024-25. The Committee appreciates the efforts of the Union Territories 
to efficiently utilize their allocated funds.  
 
5.1.4 The Committee feels that people’s representatives in a democratic setup are well 
acquainted with the priorities, needs and aspirations of the people of their constituency. They 
can play an important role in representing the needs of the local population of their 
constituencies to the UT Administration. The Administration of UTs can seek participation of 
the people’s representatives of the Union Territories while planning and implementation of 
developmental projects of the UTs.

5.2.2 The Committee has been informed that the increased allocation would be utilized, as under:

Observation/ Recommendation 
 
5.2.3 The Committee notes that water supply, power, medical and public health, roads and 
bridges are some of the schemes where allocation have been enhanced for the Financial Year 
2025-26 as compared to the Financial Year 2024-25. The Committee is of the view that these 
schemes are very important for the infrastructural development of the UT and making life 
easier for the local population. The Committee observes that allocation of budget is the basic 
step towards implementation of a scheme, but without regular and periodic monitoring of the 
projects status, the budget can remain underutilized. The Committee, therefore, recommends 
that the UT administration should ensure implementation of the schemes/projects included in 
the BE 2025-26 and the utilization of entire allocation within the Financial year. 
 
(i) Air Connectivity 
 
5.2.4 Andaman and Nicobar Islands are the largest archipelago system in the Bay of Bengal which 
is situated thousands of Kilometers away from the mainland of India. It is connected to the mainland 
through air and water route.The Committee enquired about the proposal to enhance the capacity of airport in Port Blair amid increased tourism in the UT and proposals for establishment of new airports in the UT to improve connectivity of far flung islands of the UT with mainland. The Ministry has informed that a new Integrated Terminal Building (NITB) at Veer Savarkar 
International Airport, Sri Vijaya Puram, has been made operational since August 2023. The annual 

61 
 
passenger handling capacity has increased from 0.75 Million Passengers Per Annum (MPPA) to 5 
million MPPA.  
 
5.2.5 In addition, Helicopter and Dornier Aircraft services are also available. Further, under RCS-
UDAN, 18 seater small fixed wing aircrafts are expected to be operationalized from the airports at 
Sibpur, Car Nicobar and Campbell Bay by March, 2025. 
 
5.2.6 During the course of meeting of the Committee held on 21st February, 2025, the 
representatives of UT of A&NI informed that approx. 3,32,000 tourist  came to the A&N islands 
during 2023 and it was increased to 7,21,000 tourists in the year 2024. The A&N Islands are 
connected to 18 destinations from different parts of the country through flight operations. 
International flight to Malaysia has also been in operation. 
 
Observation / Recommendation 
 
5.2.7 The Committee notes that a new Integrated Terminal Building (NITB) at Veer 
Savarkar International Airport, Sri Vijaya Puram has been made operational since August 
2023 and the annual passenger handling capacity of the airport has increased from 0.75 
Million Passengers Per Annum (MPPA) to 5 million MPPA. The Committee feels that 
commencement of international flights from A&N Islands must have also given a boost to the tourism sector by attracting more number of foreign tourists. The Committee would like to be apprised about the impact of development in tourism sector on revenue generation in the UT and on socio economic growth of the local people of the A&N Islands. The benefits of economic growth in the UT must also percolate to the local population as they equally deserve to reap those benefits. The Committee also feels that 
there are a number of islands in the UT of Andaman and Nicobar Islands which are not 
inhabited but have beautiful beaches and potential for eco tourism. The Committee 
recommends that UT administration may explore the opportunity of development of the 
tourism related activities in these islands and develop them as tourist destination for the 
growing number of tourists in the UT. 
 
(ii) Power consumption and Renewable Energy 
 
5.2.8 The information was sought on the status of power consumption and power supply in the UT 
of Andaman and Nicobar islands. It has been informed that all inhabited islands in UT of A&N 
Islands are electrified. The available capacity is 99.55 MW (including Solar & Hydro sources) 
against the power demand of 65 MW. Renewable energy sources constituted ~10.35% of total 
generation (382.48 MU) during FY 2023-24. Further, feasibility study for Solar & Wind has been 
conducted by The Energy and Resources Institute (TERI) and National Institute of Wind Energy (NIWE) 
respectively to harness Renewable Energy. 
 
5.2.9  The Committee is of the view that the UT of A&N Islands has huge potential for the 
generation of electricity through renewable energy sources like solar energy, wind energy and hydro 
energy, to cater to the need of the islands of the UT. The Committee sought to know from the UT of 
A&N Islands about the measures that have been taken and proposed to be taken to harness 
renewable sources of energy in the UT. The UT of A&N Islands replied that the A&N Islands 
administration is undertaking various measures to harness renewable sources of energy viz. Solar 
Roof Top (RTS) in Govt. buildings, Grid connected Solar Rooftop, RTS in RESCO Mode in 

62 
 
Residential buildings, Ground Mounted Solar PV projects with BESS, Floating Solar PV project, 
Wind Turbine Generator (WTG) etc. It has been also informed that enhanced allocation under the 
sub head ‘Power’ for the upcoming fiscal year is proposed to be utilized as below: 
 
1. Implementation of PM Surya Ghar Muft Bijli Yojana 
2. Payment for HSD towards power generation 
3. Power Purchase 
4. Procurement of Spare Parts, Lubricant oil 
5. Procurement of DG Sets / Transformer 
 
Observation / Recommendation 
 
5.2.10 The Committee notes that renewable energy sources constituted only 10.35 % of total 
power generation during FY 2023-24, which is very low. The Committee should be informed 
about the outcome of feasibility study for Solar & Wind energywhich has been conducted by 
The Energy and Resources Institute (TERI) and National Institute of Wind Energy (NIWE) 
respectively to harness Renewable Energy. The Committee, therefore, recommends that 
Ministry of Home Affairs along with the UT administration may approach the concerned 
Ministry/ Department to bring the new proposals of producing electricity through renewable 
energy resources at the earliest, to reduce the dependence on non-renewable sources of energy 
and preserve the fragile island from the negative impact of climate change. 
 
(iii) Forestry and Wildlife 
 
5.2.11 As informed by the Ministry, the details of various projects and scheme to be initiated under 
sub-head “Forestry & Wildlife” in BE 2025-26 is as below: 
1. Construction of animal Quarantine Centre at Biological park, Chidiyatapu.  
2. Construction of check dam/ check weir in interior forest areas for water conservation 
and wildlife. 
3. Development of eco hut, watch towers and eco-tourism facilities under various forest 
divisions 
4. Construction of Protection Camp under various Ranges. 
5. Improvement and widening of road including construction of toe wall wherever 
necessary from Biological Park to Mundapahar at Chidiyatapu. 
6. Construction of various other infrastructure assets such as office, quarters etc. 
Observation / Recommendation 
5.2.12 The Committee notes that the various projects and schemes to be initiated in the UT of 
Andaman & Nicobar Islands in Financial Year 2025-26 under the Forestry and Wildlife Sector 
are related to construction works. There are not many activities relating to wildlife 
conservation. The UT has very rich wildlife as well as marine life. Giant leatherback Sea turtle 
is one of the species which is facing the threat of extinction in the UT. The Committee feels that 
enough hatcheries should be maintained to protect eggs of sea turtles. Efforts must also be 
made to have regular coordination and collaboration with concerned organizations for 
protecting natural habitat and nesting grounds of sea turtles.  
 

63

Observation / Recommendation 
 
5.2.16 The Committee notes that the allocation of ₹503.92 crores has been made in BE 2025-26 
for Shipping and it is proposed to be utilized for the construction of High Speed Emergency 
response Vessel., chartering of passenger vessels, procurements of boats and maintenance and 
management and of vessels. The Committee also notes that during the Financial Year 2024-25 
(upto 31.12.2024), an expenditure of ₹239 crores has been incurred on the payment released to 
SCI towards operation and maintenance management of Mainland, Inter Island and foreshore 
vessels. The Committee is of the view that a major portion of budgetary allocation is used for 
the operation and maintenance management of vessels so enough funds could not be available 
for the procurement of new vessels. The Committee feels traveling through waterways is one of 
the major forms of connectivity within Islands of the UT. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs along with UT administration must work out 
plans with concerned Ministries/Departments to procure small passenger vessels for enhancing 
the waterways connectivity within the islands of UT and seek additional funds from the 
Ministry of Finance for the same.  
 
5.2.17  The Committee appreciates the action of the Government agencies which were involved 
in the geographical survey of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands which revealed the existence 
of 836 islands in place of the hitherto 572 islands in the UT of A&NI and recommends that full 
potential of these islands may be explored for various economic and tourism related activities.

5.3.5 In the meeting held on 21st February, 2025, while examining the Demands of Grants (2025-
26) of the UTs, the UT administration of Chandigarh made a request for provision of additional 

66 
 
funds of ₹208 crores on account of saving achieved by UT administration by privatization of power. 
It was also informed that the Municipal Corporation of Chandigarh (MCC) has made significant 
progress, particularly in the areas of Solid Waste Management, Liquid Waste Management, 
Horticulture Waste Management and Sludge Management, since 2021. However, these efforts need 
to be supplemented by adequate fund. Owing to privatization of the Electricity Department, there is a 
reduction of recurring expenditure to the tune of ₹208.00cr in terms of salaries, wages, maintenance 
and commercial losses. It was proposed that the said recurring savings may be allocated to the 
Chandigarh Administration on a recurring basis as additional funds to help alleviate the aforesaid 
financial crisis. The matter will be taken up with Ministry of Finance.  
 
Observation/ Recommendation 
 
5.3.6 The Committee notes that that the UT of Chandigarh had projected a demand of 
₹7924.58 crores (Gross basis), but the Government of India has allocated ₹6983.18 crores 
(Gross basis) for the Financial Year 2025-26. There is a shortfall of ₹941.40 Cr. (Gross and Net 
Basis) as compared to the allocations made and demand projected. The Committee feels that 
such substantial shortfall in allocation can hamper the progress of ongoing infrastructure 
projects in the field of health, urban development and education as envisaged by the UT 
administration for the upcoming Financial Year. The commencement of new projects could 
also be affected by the shortage of funds as focus will be on completion of ongoing projects 
rather than initiating any new schemes. The Committee, therefore, recommends that efforts 
must be made by the UT administration to strictly monitor the progress of projects and 
utilization of funds, so that it can seek additional fund at RE stage as per revised requirement.   
 
5.3.7 The Committee notes that UT administration of Chandigarh has earmarked ₹76.60 
crores for the infrastructure development in technical and higher education sector and 
Government schools for the Financial Year 2025-26. The Committee recommends that efforts 
must be initiated by the concerned agencies to adhere to the completion of 
construction/extension of the proposed buildings at the earliest.  
 
5.3.8  The Committee is of the view that in any school availability of fundamental facilities is 
paramount to fostering a conducive learning environment. These facilities not only ensure the 
safety and well-being of students but also contribute significantly to their academic growth and 
overall development. Improvement of infrastructure should be topmost priority for the 
education sector. The Committee recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs may provide 
sufficient funds to the UT administration at RE stage for the education sector for improvement 
of basic facilities like sufficient number of classrooms, toilets, libraries, laboratories and sports 
facilities at city Government schools. Measures should also be taken towards creation of smart 
classrooms by digitizing the existing ones and provision of nutritious food for mid day meals.  
  
5.3.9 The Committee was informed that the teachers and parents are slightly agitated over 
some issues related to school education in the Union Territory of Chandigarh. The Committee 
recommends that the Administration may consult with all the stakeholders involved and 
address their issues they are facing in a positive way.  
 
5.3.10 The Committee sought to know about the reasons for reduction in the allocation under sub 
head "Power and Renewable Energy" from ₹1039.62 crores in BE 2024-25 to ₹ 917.87 crores in BE 

67 
 
2025-26. The UT of Chandigarh informed that the allocation has been reduced due to the ongoing 
reforms in Power Sector. Further, after meeting the pending liability on actual basis for the year 
2024-25, the balance amount will be requested for re-appropriation to meet other urgent committed 
liabilities.   
 
5.3.11 The Committee was further informed that the details of the expenditure incurred during the 
last three years are as under:-

Observation/Recommendation 
 
5.3.13 The Committee notes that the UT administration of Chandigarh has been incurring 
huge expenditure on account of purchasing electricity. The Committee recommends that the 
Administration of Chandigarh should take measures to focus on renewable energy sources to 
reduce the demand for purchasing of power from various agencies. Further, energy 
production through renewable energy resources can be a step in the direction of green and 
clean smart city.

consumption is one of the most important indices that decide the development of a region. 
Access to electricity round the clock in each and every household is a major step in this 
direction. It would lead to the rapid economic development of the UT.  The Committee would 
like to be apprised about the round the clock availability of electricity in the UT and the 
measures that are taken by the UT Administration towards the tapping the renewable energy 
resources to meet the demands of power generation.

(ii) Tourism 
 
5.4.8 The information was sought to know about the measures taken by the UT of Dadra and 
Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu in the tourism sector. It has been informed that several key 
initiatives have been taken to improve tourism in the UT, which are as under: 
 Development of world-class seafronts, riverfronts and tent cities, 
 Diversified Tourism Experiences through new attractions and activities like adventure sports, 
aviary, development of lake fronts and public promenades.  
 Hosting of vibrant Events and Festivals such as the Monsoon Festival, musical concerts, the 
Beach Kite Festival etc. and  
 Active Online Promotion through the official website and on various social media platforms. 
5.4.9 The allocation of ₹ 56.09 crores made for the Tourism in the financial year 2025-26will be 
utilized for the following proposals: 
1) Construction of Eco tourism resort with site development in Dudhani Resort 
2) Construction of Jampore Ghat with extension of Jampore sea front upto varliwad school at 
Jampore, Moti Daman. 
 
Observation/ Recommendation 
5.4.10 The Committee is of the view that UT of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu 
has scenic and serene beauty. Its pristine beaches, colonial-era architecture and unique blend 
of Indian and Portuguese culture has the potential to attract tourists like other coastal Union 
Territories, if given enough promotion and publicity. The Committee recommends that 
measures should be taken by the UT administration of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman 
and Diu to promote organizing regional festivals, showcasing cultural heritage through such 
festivals, events showcasing traditional arts and crafts, creation of homestay facilities, etc. 
Further, exploring the scope of adventure activities and promoting adventure tour operators 
can also give impetus to tourism sector in the UT. These initiatives will not only boost tourism 
in the UT but can contribute towards creation of local employment opportunities in UT.

are posted in these wellness and primary health centres. Contractual employees are also engaged. 
Further, Shri Vinoba Bhave Civil Hospital, Silvassa has 650 Beds capacity where 98 doctors are 
working. It was also informed that NAMO Medical Education & Research Institute in Silvassa has 
various Graduate and Post Graduate courses and Undergraduate Courses, Postgraduate Course and 
Research Facilities are available in the Institute. In MBBS, 142 seats against annual intake of 177 
seats are reserved for students of UT of DadraandNagarHaveli and Daman and Diu. The existing 
Hospital in Daman is 10 bedded co-located facilities and the existing Hospital in Diu is 6 bedded co-
located facilities. Further, a 130 bedded AYUSH Hospital and Wellness Centre is under progress.

5.5 Demand No. 55 –Ladakh 
 
5.5.1 The UT has informed that out of the allocated amount of ₹4692.15crores in BE 2025-26, ₹ 
2450.00 crores is Revenue Component and ₹2242.15 crores is Capital component. Out of ₹ 2450.00 
crores under Revenue component, ₹1771.51 Crores will be utilized for Salary, allowances and LTC 
and remaining will be utilized  for other major revenue heads such as ORE, Subsidies and 
Professional Services, Fuels and Lubricants, Material and Supplies and Office Expenses etc., which 
are essential to run the entablements of the UT of Ladakh. The allocation or ₹2242.15 crores under 
Capital component will be utilized for already approved schemes where the works are being 
completed. 
 
5.5.2 During the meeting of the Committee held on 21st February, 2025, the UT administration of 
Ladakh informed the Committee that road connectivity and telecom connectivity are two important 
activities. Total road length is around 6700 kms. Since the creation of UT of Ladakh efforts are 
being made to focus on roads and bridges. Of the total 134 roads and 32 bridges, 40 have been 
completed. 
 
5.5.3 The budget allocation under the sub head Power has been reduced from ₹189.25 crores in BE 
2024-25 to ₹84.64 crores in BE 2025-26. It has been informed that the allocated Budget of under 
sub-head Power under the revenue component will be utilized for purchase of power and running of 
Diesel Generator Stations in the far-flung areas of Zanskar, Nubra and Changthang. Whereas, the 
allocations of the capital component shall be utilized on the on-going approved works and schemes 
under the capital heads of Power Department. In view of the reduced allocations, the completion of 
the ongoing projects will be prioritized. Since there are considerable reductions in the allocation for 
the financial year 2025-26 vis a vis BE 2024-25, the allocations are truly insufficient. Therefore, the 
allocation provided for FY 2025-26 will need to be utilized optimally to accommodate the allocation.  
The UT may project additional requirement at the RE Stage or seek supplementary grants, if 
required. 
 
5.5.4 In order to harness the renewable resources of energy in the UT of Ladakh, the following 
projects have been taken up:  
i. 11 GW project is being implemented by SECI in UT of Ladakh.  
ii. Two projects of capacity 25 MW & 12 MW have been awarded. Work will start in 
March 2025 on these projects. 
iii. Power Development Department has submitted proposal to Ministry of New and 
Renewable Energy (MNRE) for approval for covering a target of 10,000 rooftops 
through Renewable Energy Service Company (RESCO) mode. 
iv. 1533 Government buildings are being covered with solar rooftops in Capex mode.  
v. Proposal for 4 mini-Hydel projects under 25 MW have been submitted to MNRE for 
funding. 
 
 
 
 

73 
 
Observation /Recommendation 
 
 5.5.5 The Committee observes that the fund allocations for the Union Territory of 
Ladakh has been reduced for the Financial Year 2025-26 to ₹ 4692.15 crores (Net basis) as 
compared to allocation of ₹5958.00 crores in the Financial Year 2024-25. The Committee feels 
that the reduction in budgetary allocation can hamper the ongoing works and schemes of the 
Union Territory. The Committee recommends that the Ministry of Home affairs may need to 
pursue the additional fund requirement with Ministry of Finance at RE stage if the UT of 
Ladakh seeks additional funds at later stage.  
 
5.5.6 The Union Territory of Ladakh has immense power potential in the solar and wind 
energy sector due to its high altitude and bright sky and dry weather condition. The 
Committee appreciates that the UT administration has taken up various projects to harness 
the renewable resources of energy. The Committee would like to be apprised about the 
percentage of power generated from the solar energy sector and initiatives taken by the UT 
Administration to generate power from wind resource.   
 
(i) Employment opportunities  
 
5.5.7 The Ministry informed about the steps taken by the UT of Ladakh to promote local 
employment opportunities in Ladakh especially in Tourism sector which are enlisted below:   
 
a. Notification of “Ladakh Homestay Policy 2023”.  
b. Notification of Ladakh Mountaineering Policy 2024.  
c. Registration and Renewal Guidelines for Adventure Tour Operators and Adventure Activity 
Guidelines.   
d. Organizing Regional Festivals.  
e. Training Youth through State Institute of Hotel Management (SIHM) Diploma Programs.  
f. Participation in Travel Trade Shows to boost tourism.  
 
5.5.8 Steps taken to promote local employment opportunities in Ladakh especially in Handicraft’s 
sector are enlisted below:   
 
a. Providing opportunities in entrepreneurship development by imparting skill development 
training in various crafts through departmental level training centres and through esteemed 
institutions like National Institute of Design (NID), National Institute of Fashion 
Technology (NIFT), Obeetee Carpet Company and many others for local business building 
opportunities. 
b. Providing platform for Marketing of products made by the craftspeople at local and national 
level events and fairs like India International Trade Fair (IITF), Bharat Parv, Jammu 
Mahotsav, National Handloom Day, Enchanting Ladakh at Dilli Haat, Punjab International 
Trade Expo (PITEX), etc.   
c. Conducting special workshops about digital marketing, social media presence, photography 
for the artisans to build their marketing and digital presence in accordance with 
contemporary needs, through institutes like NID. 
d. Providing end to end support to the artisans and craft persons through subsidized loans, tools 
and equipment for expanding and building their businesses through the PM Vishwakarma 
scheme.  

74 
 
e. Opening of the Brand Ladakh showrooms in Leh market and Delhi with the objective to 
promote Ladakhi products and to provide opportunity to the local entrepreneurs to promote 
their products and increase their sales.  
f. Organization of events throughout the calendar year on occasions such as Handloom Day to 
boost the sales of the local entrepreneurs, which provide them a major fillip to the aspiring 
artisans and young entrepreneurs. 
 
Observation/ Recommendation 
 
5.5.9 The Committee notes the steps taken by the UT administration of Ladakh for 
promoting employment opportunities for local youth in the region. Providing end to end 
support to the artisans and crafts persons through subsidized loans, tools and equipment for 
expanding and building their businesses through the PM Vishwakarma scheme is one such 
scheme which can give boost to self sustainable small scale entrepreneurship in the UT. 
Ladakh is a high yielding pashmina and wool grower district and it involved various crafts 
like weaving, spinning, dying, tailoring and knitting. UT administration must ensure that 
enough financial as well as administrative and skill development support must be provided to 
the local artisans and craftsmen for the preservation of the traditional craftsmanship of 
Ladakh and upliftment of artisans and weavers of Ladakh.  
 
(ii) Recruitment Initiatives 
 
5.5.10 The Ministry has informed that there are 1275 vacancies in Gazetted posts and 3596 
vacancies in non-Gazetted posts in various departments of the UT of Ladakh. Following steps have 
been taken to fill the vacant posts: 
 
Recruitment to non-Gazetted posts 
a. After formation of the UT of Ladakh recruitment to 3172 posts have been done through 
various recruitment agencies like Staff Selection Commission (SSC), Autonomous Hill 
Development Subordinate Services Recruitment Board, Leh & Kargil, Ladakh Police Board. 
b. Staff Selection Board namely “Ladakh Subordinate Service Staff Selection Board 
(LSSSSB)” has been set up in the UT of Ladakh. The remaining vacant non-Gazetted posts 
will be filled by the LSSSSB.  
 
Recruitment on Gazetted posts: 
 
i. Recruitment Rules for 75 categories of Gazetted posts, aggregating a total of 1142 posts have 
been framed. 
 
Reservation policy for local youth:  
 
(i) Reservation in appointment for various posts in Union territory of Ladakh is governed under the 
Jammu and Kashmir Reservation Act, 2004 and the Jammu and Kashmir Reservation Rules, 
2005.The UT Administration vide S.O. 14 dated: 23.04.2020 has amended the J&K Reservation 
Rules, 2005, providing for the following proportion of reservation to various categories:  
 
(a) forty-five (45) per cent for Scheduled Tribes (STs),  

75 
 
(b) one (1) per cent for Scheduled Castes (SCs),  
(c) four (4) per cent for residents of area adjoining the Line of Actual Control (ALC) and  
(d) ten(10) per cent for Economically Weaker Sections (EWS)  
 
Besides, horizontal reservation of six (6) per cent is available to Ex-servicemen and three (3) percent 
for physical challenged persons. 
 
Observation/Recommendation 
 
5.5.11 The Committee notes that there are 1275 vacancies in Gazetted posts and 3596 
vacancies in non-Gazetted posts in various departments of the UT of Ladakh. Although steps 
have been taken by the UT administration to fill the vacant posts, the Committee feels number 
of vacancies are huge in the UT and shortage of officers and staff in various UT administration 
can affect the smooth and seamless functioning of the administration. The Committee 
recommends that the process of recruitment to fill the vacant posts must be sped up so that the 
educated young generation of the UT should not be bereft of employment opportunities in the 
UT itself.   
 
(iii) Availability of Electricity 
 
5.5.12 Following steps have been taken toensure year-round availability of electricity: 
 
i. To improve round the year power availability, the UT of Ladakh is implementing various 
Centrally Sponsored schemes/ projects and projects under UT Budget.  
ii. The Revamped Distribution Sector Scheme (RDSS) with a cost of Rs. 1200.00 Cr is being 
implemented to extend the grid power to Changthang and Border Areas of Ladakh which are 
presently supplied through Diesel Generators. 220 KV Line is being extended from Phyang 
to Disket Nubra and Drass to Padum Zanskar so that the off-grid area of Nubra and Zanskar 
are also grid connected.  
iii. The Solar Energy Corporation of India (SECI) is setting up a 25 MW AC solar project at 
Taru under PMDP-2015. The LPDD is also setting up a 12 MW, AC Solar Project at Phey 
and setting up of 1.5 MW AC Solar Project along with Battery Energy Storage Systems 
(BESS) in Nubrahas been approved.  
iv. A 9.00 MW Dah and 9.00 MW Hanu Hydro Electric Projects are under construction and 
scheduled for commissioning in 2025. 
v. Besides, under the UT Budget, the UT of Ladakh is also taking up creation of Distribution 
Transformer (DT) stations, New HT and LT infrastructures and augmentation works for 
providing and improving power supply to consumers.  
 
(iv) Availability of potable water 
 
5.5.13 Following steps have been taken to ensure year-round availability of potable water: 
 
i. At present 96.05% households in rural areas are being saturated under the scheme JalJeevan 
Mission (JJM) by providing potable drinking water facility through Functional Household 
Tap Connections (FHTCs). Presently, potable water is being supplied through Public Stand 
Post, handpumps and through Water tankers. 

76 
 
ii. Further, in Urban areas, potable drinking water are supplied through scheme of Urban 
Infrastructure Development Scheme for Small and Medium Towns (UIDSSMT). Water is 
also supplied through water tankers during harsh winters. 
iii. Besides, to augment the drinking water supply to the Leh and Kargil towns under 24x7 Water 
Supply Scheme, a 8 MLD Water Treatment Plant at Choglamsar Leh is in progress under 
Special Development Programme (SDP) at an estimated cost of 19.00 Cr and will be 
completed by April, 2025.  DPRs have been submitted to Ministry of Housing and Urban 
Affairs (MoHUA), Govt. of India for setting up of 11 MLD plant at an estimated cost of Rs. 
390.00 crores for Leh town. 
5.5.14 Similarly, for Kargil town, two Water Treatment Plant projects each of 8 MLD at an 
estimated cost of ₹410.70 crores is being constructed at Kargil under SDP and AMRUT 2.0, the 
DPR being submitted to MoHUA. 
 
Observation/Recommendation 
 
5.5.15 The Committee appreciates the efforts taken by the UT Administration to augment the 
drinking water supply to the Leh and Kargil towns under 24x7 Water Supply Scheme  and 
providing potable drinking water facility through Functional Household Tap Connections 
(FHTCs) to the rural households. The Committee recommends that the Government may 
extend necessary funds and resources to cover the remaining rural areas as well, at the earliest, 
to make potable water available to all the households. 
 
5.5.16 The Committee recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs along with UT 
administration of Ladakh must pursue with the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs 
(MoHUA), Govt. of India for setting up of 11 MLD plant for which Detailed Project Reports 
(DPRs) have been submitted at an estimated cost of ₹390.00 crores for Leh town. 
 
(v) Waste Disposal and Management 
5.5.17 To improve the infrastructure for waste disposal and management, the UT of Ladakh has 
taken following steps: 
Urban Areas: 
i. Solid Waste Segregation and Collection: A comprehensive door-to-door waste collection 
system across both Leh and Kargil Towns has been implemented. All streets and main 
bazaars in Leh and Kargil, with a frequency of twice a day are being swept. 
 
ii. Development of Waste Management Facilities: An Integrated Solid Waste Management 
Plant in Leh with a capacity of 30 TPD (20 TPD for dry waste and 10 TPD for wet waste) 
became operational in 2020 to process both types of waste separately.  Construction of Solid 
Waste Management Plant in Kargil with a capacity of 25 TPD (15 TPD for dry waste and 10 
TPD for wet waste) which was completed in November 2024 is currently operating on a trial 
basis. 
 
iii. Liquid Waste Management: A 3 MLD (Million Liters per Day) Sewage Treatment Plant is 
already operational in Agling, Leh, and  the capacity of the plant will be increased upto 10.5 
MLD. In Kargil to address future population growth. 
 

77 
 
iv. Legacy Waste Management: The legacy waste at Bombgarh in Leh, which accumulated to 
approximately 58,910 tons, has been successfully remediated. 
 
v. Policy Framework and Governance: A comprehensive ban on single-use plastics in Leh 
and Kargil Towns have been implemented. Installation of Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) has 
been made mandatory for all established guest houses, hotels and building with more than 10 
rooms to ensure effective wastewater management. A series of policies including Solid 
Waste Management, Plastic Smart Ladakh Strategy, and the Strategy on Construction and 
Demolition Waste have been introduced.  On directions from National Green Tribunal 
(NGT), a ban on open burning of municipal and plastic waste has been imposed.  Solid 
Waste Management Bye-laws has been notified in Leh and Kargil. 
 
Rural Areas:  
i. Solid Waste Collection Centers and Plastic waste Segregation centers are in place in all the 
31 blocks of UT of Ladakh.  
ii. Door to door waste collection is being done which is facilitated by the fleet of 35 waste 
collecting vehicles.  
iii. Community Sanitary Complexes have been constructed in almost all the villages and 
especially at the places where tourist footfall is high and at places where people throng for 
religious pilgrimage/gathering. 
iv. Biodigesters have been installed at important tourist/religious places. 
v. Integrated Household Latrines have been given to all the eligible beneficiaries.  
vi. The UT of Ladakh has achieved 100% Grey Water Management in all rural areas. 
vii. Out of 240 villages, 203 villages have been declared as Open Defection Free Plus (ODF 
Plus) Model Villages. 
5.5.18 In addition to the above, Regular Information, Education, and Communication (IEC) 
activities, such as workshops and murals, are being conducted to educate tourists and locals about 
responsible waste management and reducing single-use plastics. 
 
Observation/Recommendation 
 
5.5.19 The Committee notes the various steps taken for solid waste management in Ladakh 
like implementation of a comprehensive door-to-door waste collection system across Leh and 
Kargil Towns and construction of Solid Waste Management Plant in Kargil with a capacity of 
25 TPD (Tonnes per Day) and is of the view that the UT administration is doing commendable 
job in segregation and management of waste disposal. The Committee recommends that the 
UT Administration must ensure that the comprehensive ban on single-use plastics in Leh and 
Kargil towns must be strictly adhere to which will help in reducing land and water pollution in 
Ladakh. 
 
(vi) Health 
 
5.5.20 The Budgetary allocations under the sub head “Medical and Public Health” is ₹68.49 crores 
in BE 2025-26. It has been informed that the budgetary allocations under this sub head for Revenue 
Component is mostly under Other Revenue Expenditure and Material and Supplies. The allocation 
will be utilized for the heating charges of two district Hospitals, 18 PHCs and 6 CHCs, General 

78 
 
Nursing and Midwifery (GNM) School and for the procurement of Medical Supplies and essential 
drugs etc., whereas the allocation under the Capital component for the F.Y 2025-26 shall be utilized 
for the completion of 03 ongoing works at SNM Hospital Leh. 
 
Observation/Recommendation 
 
5.5.21 The Committee was informed that the UT administration is giving all out support to the 
people to give maximum health care facilities. Last year the mother mortality rate was zero in 
the UT of Ladakh mainly because the pregnant mothers are admitted to the hospitals for 
delivery and deliveries are taking place in the hospitals. The Committee appreciates the 
achievement of the UT administration in this regard. The Committee suggests and 
recommends that the experiences and practices adopted by the UT of Ladakh in the health 
sector can be followed in other UTs and States also.

for which an allocation ₹951.00 crores has been made to the UT of Delhi by the Union 
Government, so that these projects can fully utilize the fund allocation and avoid cost overruns 
and strictly adhere to their completion time line.  
 
(i) Waste Clearance from landfill sites 
 
5.7.5 The Ministry has informed that as intimated by the Municipal Corporation of Delhi, the 
target for clearance of waste from Bhalswa and Ghazipur landfill sites is December 2028. To prevent 
the release of toxic gases from these sites to the adjoining residential areas, the toxic gases are 
released through the high-density polyethylene (HDPE) pipes.  As regards leachate generation, the 
same is being trapped through temporary drain and are not allowed to flow in the adjoining 
residential areas.
