5.7.7 The implementation of this project will improve water supply services in Chandrawal WTP 
command area located in Central Delhi and covering area of about 96 sq/km i.e. about 6.5% of Delhi 
area. The project will improve water supply system for population of 22 lakh i.e. about 11% of Delhi 
population. 
 
Observation/ Recommendation 
 
5.7.8 The Committee notes that Chandrawal Water Treatment Plant project is proposed to 
be executed in six packages and expected to be completed by September, 2027. Earlier, the 
project was expected to be completed in October, 2024. This shows that the completion of the 
project has been delayed. Considering the shortage of water in different parts of Delhi, the 
Committee observes that the project is vital for Delhi as it will help in improving water supply 
system for population of 22 lakh i.e. about 11% of Delhi population. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the MHA may take up with the UT of Delhi for periodic monitoring of the 
progress of work so that the timelines are met without cost overruns. Adequate funds may be 
sought from the Ministry of Finance as per the requirement.  
 
5.7.9   While discussing DFG (2025-26) of the Union Territories in the meeting held on 21st 
February, 2025, it was observed by the Committee that a large number of private hostels for boys 
and girls are being run in Delhi. Similarly, various coaching institutes are also being run in Delhi 
without any clear guidelines and regulations. Further during the meeting, it was also noted that there 
is around 25-30 Lakhs floating population in Delhi.  
 
Observation/ Recommendation 
 
5.7.10 The Committee is of view that a substantial number of private coaching centres and 
hostels for boys and girls are being run in Delhi without any strict norms and regulations. 
These are not providing adequate space and basic living conditions to the young population 
who come from far-flung areas for fulfilling their future employment aspirations.  The 
Committee would like to be apprised about the number of such coaching centres and hostels 
that are being run in Delhi and what kind to accommodation and other basic facilities are 
being provided by them to the students and residents of these facilities. The Committee may 
also be informed about the provision of licensing of such centres and the last inspection done to 
review the conditions of these centres and hostels.  
 

86 
 
5.7.11 The Committee also like to be informed about the maintenance of any kind of record of 
around 25-30 Lakh floating population in the region of Delhi and what kind of basic facilities 
are available for them. 
 
5.8 Demand No. 58 - Transfers to Jammu & Kashmir 
 
5.8.1 An amount of Rs. 41000.07 crore has been allocated for UT of Jammu & Kashmir in BE 
2025-26. The UT Administration plan to utilize this allocation as under: 
 
i) Rs. 39306.30 crore is the Central Assistance to Union Territory is Rs. (in lieu of Revenue 
Deficit Grant & Share in Central Taxes) will be utilised for meeting salary, pension, 7th CPC 
Allowances and other revenue component deficit.  
ii) Rs. 279 crore is the grant towards contribution to Union Territory Disaster Response Fund 
and shall be utilised for mitigation of unforeseen events and disasters.  
iii) Rs. 1313 crore is the grant towards Urban Local Bodies and PRIs shall be utilized for 
creation of urban and rural assets. 
iv) Rs. 101.77 crore is the grants for creation of capital assets. 
5.8.2 During the meeting of the Committee held on 21st February, 2025 while discussing DFG 
(2025-26) of the Union Territory of Jammu and Kashmir, the Committee was informed  that 
investment of ₹ 61,528 crore being made in road connectivity,  5 National Highways, 2 Ring Roads, 
and Delhi -Amritsar -Katra Expressway are under execution and 99% eligible rural habitations are 
connected. Jammu Srinagar Highway is nearing completion, which can substantially reduce travel 
time. Train service has been extended to Kashmir valley with a project cost of ₹ 37,000 crores. It 
was also informed that evening flights have been made operational and air traffic in Srinagar airport 
has been doubled in last five years.  
 
5.8.3 During the meeting is was also informed that Hydro Power Generation will be doubled by 
2026 with addition of 04 hydro Power generation projects of 3014 MWs. Transmission capacity has 
been increased by 50% (4239 MVAs) through completion of 28 projects. Distribution capacity has 
been increased by 40% (5708 MVAs) through completion of 194 projects and there is focus on Solar 
Renewable Energy.  
 
5.8.4 In the sector of provision of drinking water 81% (15.54 lakh) Rural Households have been 
provided Functional Household Tap Connections (FHTC), 1415 schemes completed under Jal 
Jeevan Mission (JJM) and remaining are under progress. Further, work on Shahpur-Kandi Dam 
Project has been commenced. The work is nearing completion and J&K may receive 1150 cusecs 
water & 20% of electricity generated annually at low cost, irrigation potential of more than 32,000 
hectares will be created. 
5.8.5 During the meeting, strategy for the displaced people of Kashmir was also discussed. It was 
informed that for the Welfare of Kashmiri Migrants various measures have been undertaken by the 
Government which include: 
1. Providing of cash assistance and Ration  
2. Shelter 
3.  Health insurance coverage ABPMJAY-Sehat, education and sanitation facilities 4. Onlineservices 5. Employment 

87 
 
6. Transit accommodation to PM package employees 
 
Observation/ Recommendation 
5.8.6 The Committee notes that the UT administration of Jammu and Kashmir has achieved 
successful and nearing completion of various projects in the sectors of road, rail and air 
connectivity, power generation and provision of drinking water in rural households. The 
Committee feels empowerment in infrastructure projects creates conducive environment for 
investments in industries and create opportunities for entrepreneurship in underdeveloped 
regions. The Committee recommends that UT Administration should take measures to 
monitor the progress of ongoing projects in these sectors and speed up the projects which are 
in pipeline.  
 
 (i) Tourism 
 
5.8.7 As reported by UT of Jammu and Kashmir, the Tourism Department, Jammu & Kashmir has 
undertaken several measures to upgrade and expand existing tourism units and boost the tourism 
sector: 
1. The G20 Working Group Meeting on Tourism was organized in Srinagar.  
2. The improved security has led to phenomenal increase in tourists arrivals.  
3. Events such as the Gulmarg Winter Festival, Pahalgam Snow Festival and Sonmarg Adventure 
Carnival have been introduced to attract tourists and promote local culture. 
4. Tentageaccommodation are being promoted at various tourist destinations such as Gurez, Sonamarg, Jai, Pahalgam, Gulmarg, Kishtwar (Machail), Sonamarg etc. 
5. J&K is emerging as Winter Sports Capital of the Country. Three events have been organized in 
the Gulmarg World famous winter destination since March, 2020.  
6. 4th Edition Khelo India Winter Games was held at Gulmarg, Kashmir from 21st to 25th 
February, 2024  
7. Gulmarg Gondola ride service was used by more than 10 lakh people during 2023. 
8. Drag lifts installed at Sonmarg and Gulmarg 
9. Renovation and upgradation of existing tourism units at various tourist destinations of Jammu 
and Kashmir region by modernizing infrastructure, enhancing facilities and ensuring strict 
adherence to the Master Plan. 
10. Development of eco-friendly facilities such as trekking trails, camping sites and waste 
management systems. 
11. Domestic and international campaigns have been launched to showcase J&K as a year-round 
destination. Roadshows, trade fairs and digital promotions are being conducted to reach global 
audiences. 
 
5.8.8  During the course of the meeting of the Committee held on 21st February, 2025, while 
discussing DFG (2025-26) of the MHA, it was informed to the Committee that all time high tourist 
visits recorded during 2024. Nine new tourist destination development proposals have been sent to 
Department of Economic Affairs, Ministry of Finance, Government of India involving multilateral 
funding with robust master plan, governance mechanism, government investment and private 
investment. Further, promotion of tourist flow to Sonmarg round the year through operationlisation 
of Sonmarg tunnel has taken place. It was also informed that 1/3rd of the Dal lake has been 
rejuvenated and 6.5 sq. kms has been cleared of lilies.

5.8.10 The Committee is heartened to note that the tourists’ arrival in J&K for the last two 
years have increased manifold. The Committee takes notes of the steps taken for the 
development and promotion of tourism in the UT of Jammu and Kashmir. The Committee is 
of the view that the tourism and hospitality sector, apart from generating large revenue, is a 
major source of livelihood for the people of J&K by creating employment opportunities and 
boosting local businesses. It also contributes towards regional and rural development of the 
UT. The Committee recommends that UT administration should chalk out a road map to take 
initiatives to position J&K as a world class tourism destination, improve the tourist experience 
and create sustainable economic opportunities for the local population.  
 
(ii) Promotion of local employment opportunities  
5.8.11 The Department of Tourism, Jammu & Kashmir Government, introduced Homestay Policy-
2022 wherein residential houses can be converted into Homestays, providing an alternative source of 
income and employment opportunities for rural and urban families. At present, 2302 
Homestays/PGH have been registered in rural and urban areas of Jammu and Kashmir Division. 
5.8.12 A variety of skill development programs have been initiated to train locals in tourism-related 
services like hospitality, guiding, and management. J&K is also famous for adventure tourism 
activities, such as trekking, skiing, river rafting, and mountain biking. The promotion of such 
activities has opened up employment opportunities for local guides, instructors and adventure 
operators. 
5.8.13 Opportunities in entrepreneurship development have been promoted by the department by 
imparting skill development training in various crafts through departmental level training centres and 
through esteemed institutions. Besides this, there are following achievements:

Observation/ Recommendation 
 
5.8.18 The Committee is of the view that the industrial sector in Jammu & Kashmir needs to 
be enhanced for generation of employment for the local youth. The Committee recommends 
that initiatives should be taken to improve infrastructure i.e. investing in transportation, 
power supply, and internet connectivity to make region more attractive for businesses and 
investors.  At the same time, by encouraging entrepreneurship, promoting tourism, enhancing 
education and skill development, the employment potential of Jammu and Kashmir can be 
significantly increased. Providing loans on subsidised rates can also improve employment 
scenario in the UT.  
 
5.8.19 The Committee recommends that the Administration of Jammu and Kashmir should 
take more concrete steps towards providing employment opportunities to educated youth of 
the UT. Additional initiatives should be taken for implementation of self-employment schemes 
across various sectors to establish sustainable income generating ventures for unemployed 
youth.  
 
5.8.20 Handicraft sector in the UT of Jammu and Kashmir is one of the largest employment 
generation sources in the region. The UT administration should strive for Geographical 
Indication (GI) tagging of each and every crafts of the region as it can  promote cultural 
heritage and economic growth as with GI tagged product, the producers are able to get 
significantly better prices for their products.

RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS - AT A GLANCE 
 OVERALL ASSESSMENT  OF DEMANDS FOR GRANTS (2025-26) OF THE MHA  
 
The Committee observes, based on the above-mentioned information, that the actual 
Budget Estimate (BE) for 2025-26 is lower than the projected BE for 2025-26 across several 
key areas, reflecting a more conservative allocation than initially anticipated. The total budget 
has been reduced from ₹184,236.94 crores (projected BE 2025-26) to ₹160391.06 crores (actual 
BE 2025-26), marking a 12.94% decrease. Within the Central Armed Police Forces (CAPFs), 
actual allocations fall short of projections, with reductions observed across the CRPF, NSG, 
BSF, ITBP, CISF, Assam Rifles, and SSB. This indicates a recalibration of expenditure 
priorities. Similarly, significant downward revisions have been made to allocations for the 
Intelligence Bureau (IB), NATGRID, Central Police Organizations (CPOs) and Delhi Police. 
Police infrastructure projects, particularly CAPF building projects, have experienced a 
substantial reduction from ₹6,403.02 crores to ₹4,038.70 crores, which may potentially impact 
construction of new buildings. The Committee notes that such reductions may adversely affect 
critical schemes under these heads and may slow down modernization programs as well as 
hinder the execution of developmental projects, particularly in border areas. Hence, the 
Committee recommends that the MHA may take up the matter with the Ministry of Finance to 
seek increased allocations under these heads in alignment with their original projections. This 
will ensure that the essential developmental schemes are not affected due to insufficient 
funding. The Committee hopes that funds may be demanded for various developmental and 
expansion projects at the RE stage depending on the utilization pattern. 
 
(Para 2.5.3) 
 
 The Committee observes that the significant reduction in the Grant-in-Aid to States for 
Home Guards from ₹566.01 crores in RE 2024-25 to ₹30.01 crores in BE 2025-26 is based on 
the expectation that all backlog claims will be cleared in FY 2024-25. However, the Committee 
emphasizes the need to reassess the funding requirements for new claims and ensure that 
States are adequately supported to maintain the operational efficiency of Home Guards. 
Similarly, with respect to the Holistic Development of Islands in Union Territories, where the 
allocation has been reduced from ₹88 crores in RE 2024-25 to a nominal ₹0.01 crore in BE 
2025-26, the Committee recommends expediting the preparation of EFC papers and project 
reports to ensure timely approval and funding for developmental activities in the UTs. The 
Committee further recommends closely monitoring expenditure trends and engaging with 
stakeholders to ensure that reductions across various components do not adversely impact 
service delivery or developmental goals. 
 
(Para 2.5.4) 
 
The Committee recommends the Ministry to take effective measures to address this 
issue by setting clear quarterly targets for fund utilization, enhancing planning and monitoring 
mechanisms to track progress and identifying bottlenecks in project execution at an early 
stage. The Ministry should also consider establishing a reporting mechanism to monitor 
monthly fund utilization and take corrective action wherever delays are identified. The 
Committee is of the view that by adopting these measures, the Ministry can ensure that 
allocated funds are utilized efficiently and effectively throughout the year, thereby avoiding 

94 
 
last-minute spending rushes and ensuring that the major projects are executed as planned 
without any delay.  
 (Para 2.5.5) 
 
BUDGETARY ALLOCATION (2025-26) OF DEMAND NO. (52-59) FOR UNION  
TERRIRORIES 
 
 In the meeting of the Committee held on 21st February, 2025, it was informed by the 
officials of the MHA  that the total expenditure incurred by all UTs under Demand No. 57-59 
(UTs with Legislature) against the allocation made in RE 2024-25, has been 90.91%.  The UT 
of Delhi, J&K  and Puducherry have spent 85.42%, 89.16% and 98.17% respectively, against 
the transfer made by the MHA to them during RE 2024-25.  
 
 (Para 2.6.6) 
 
 The Committee notes that there has been a decrease of 2.07% in allocations made for 
the Union Territories (UTs) in the year 2025-26 as compared to the year 2024-25. Further, 
there has been no substantial increase in the capital budget for the UTs (without legislatures) 
during the past three years. In fact, it has been slightly reduced during the Financial Year 
2025-26 as compared to Financial Year 2024-25. The Committee is of the view that to meet the 
development needs of UTs, adequate capital fund is required so that the projects pertaining to 
the development of physical and social infrastructure like roads, power, water and sanitation, 
health and education in all UTs do not suffer due to lack of sufficient funds. Further, to ensure 
that the sensitive and fragile ecology of some of the Union Territories are not affected, special 
measures will have to be taken while implementing the developmental projects. This requires 
extra expenditure which in turn demands more allocation of funds. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that the Ministry may try to address the needs of the Union Territories and any 
shortfall in capital budget may be provided at RE stage so that they could continue with their 
ongoing projects and schemes and initiate new schemes.  
 
 
(Para 2.6.7) 
 
DEMAND NO. 49 - MINISTRY OF HOME AFFAIRS 
 
OFFICIAL  LANGUAGE 
 The Committee appreciates the Ministry's initiatives, such as All India and Regional 
Official Language Conferences, for significantly advancing the use of Hindi and implementing 
the Official Language Policy effectively. The Committee is of the view that to further 
strengthen these efforts, the Ministry could consider increasing the frequency and regional 
outreach of these conferences, incorporating interactive workshops and addressing practical 
challenges faced by Government offices. The Committee commends the development of the AI-
based translation software Kanthastha, noting that its scope could be expanded by integrating 
additional Indian languages and enhancing technical translation accuracy. Further, the 
Committee recognizes the establishment of Bhartiya Bhasha Anubhag, as it aims to facilitate 
official communication between the Central and State Governments in their respective official 
languages, ensuring linguistic inclusivity in governance. The Committee appreciates that the 

95 
 
Budget under the head “official language” has been increased to the tune of more than 20% 
which may enable the Ministry toprioritize engaging technical expertise and ensuring seamless 
integration with Government communication systems. 
(Para 3.2.5) 
NATIONAL  DISASTER RESPONSE FORCE (NDRF) 
 
The Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA) informed the Committee that for FY 2025-26, the 
NDRF has been allocated ₹1,922.59 crores against a projected requirement of ₹2,098.63 crores, 
resulting in a shortfall of ₹176.04 crores. The Committee notes this gap and hopes it will not 
adversely impact the functioning of the NDRF. It appreciates the critical role of the NDRF in 
disaster response and emphasizes the need for adequate funding to address increasing 
demands, particularly for additional personnel. The Committee recommends that the MHA 
may take up the matter with the Ministry of Finance at the Revised Estimates (RE) stage to 
secure the remaining funds, ensuring that operational efficiency and preparedness are not 
compromised during emergencies. 
. 
(Para 3.3.10) 
 
The Committee notes the commendable efforts in launching the Yuva Aapda Mitra 
Scheme to train young volunteers for disaster response and recognizes its potential to 
strengthen community resilience nationwide. The Committee recommends developing 
standardized training modules that comprehensively cover essential disaster response skills, 
ensuring uniformity and effectiveness in community disaster preparedness including local 
committees, youths and youth organisation. Additionally, the Committee emphasizes the need 
for regular monitoring and evaluation of program effectiveness to identify areas for 
improvement and ensure that the approved financial outlay is efficiently allocated to support 
training and resource procurement. Further, the Committee recommends promoting the 
creation of Community Emergency Stockpiles to enhance disaster preparedness. 
 
(Para 3.3.11) 
 
The Committee emphasizes the need for the Ministry to conduct comprehensive 
research to develop a long-term roadmap for disaster resilience and adaptation, focusing 
onfuture requirements for mitigation and preparedness in light of climate change and 
increasing disaster frequency. The Committee urges the Ministry to increase investment in 
disaster-resilient infrastructure, including critical facilities such as hospitals, schools and 
transport networks, to minimize the impact of disasters and improve recovery efforts. The 
Committee recommends that the Ministry may include new and emerging disasters in its 
disaster management plans, such as those caused by heat waves etc. It further recommends 
establishing a formal mechanism for the periodicreview and update of the Notified Disasters 
list to ensure the Act remains relevant and responsive to emerging disaster risks through 
consultation with experts, stakeholders and affected communities. 
 
(Para 3.3.12) 
 

96 
 
Acknowledging that the NDRF is entirely dependent on deputation from CAPFs and 
currently faces a 21% vacancy rate, while CAPFs themselves are experiencing staffing 
shortages, the Committee recommends the Ministry to conduct a thorough review of the 
deputation policies. The Committee is of the view that the review should assess the impact of 
the current seven-year deputation tenure and explore incentives, such as flexible terms or 
enhanced allowances, to encourage more CAPF personnel to volunteer for NDRF assignments. 
Streamlining the nomination process and improving communication between NDRF and 
CAPFs are also crucial steps for expediting the filling of vacancies and ensuring the NDRF's 
operational readiness. 
 (Para 3.3.13)  
RELIEF AND REHABILITATION FOR MIGRANTS AND REPATRIATES 
 
 The Committee notes the Government's ongoing efforts to provide relief and 
rehabilitation to Kashmiri migrants, West Pakistan Refugees and Bru migrants and 
emphasizes the importance of maintaining momentum in these crucial endeavours. The 
Committee recommends the completion of the remaining Government job positions for 
Kashmiri migrants and expediting the construction of the remaining transit accommodations. 
The Committee appreciates the Ministry for the effective integration of Kashmiri/Jammu 
migrants into the National Food Security Act (NFSA) database, as it ensures their inclusion in 
Government welfare schemes.  
 (Para 3.4.6) 
 Regarding the Bru migrant resettlement in Tripura, the Committee commends the 
progress achieved and recommends sustained support to ensure the completion of housing 
construction, the provision of financial aid and the availability of skill development 
opportunities, fostering their integration and socio-economic well-being. The Committee also 
recommends enhancing awareness drives to ensure all migrant and refugee groups are fully 
aware of and can access various Government schemes, such as self-employment programs, 
social welfare initiatives and healthcare benefits and streamlining the online platform for 
issuing essential certificates.  
 (Para 3.4.7) 
 
FREEDOM FIGHTERS (PENSION AND OTHER BENEFITS) 
 The Committee notes the decline in the number of beneficiaries due to the advanced age 
of freedom fighters and acknowledges the Government's efforts in simplifying the scheme 
through policy amendments and linking the portal with Registrar General of India (RGI). The 
Committee appreciates the issuance of a Standard Operating Procedure for all District 
Collectors to regularly engage with aged freedom fighters.  
(Para 3.5.2) 
HELICOPTOR SERVICES  
 The Committee notes the reduction in budget allocation for helicopter services due to 
decreased demand from State and UT Governments and acknowledges the critical role these 

97 
 
services play in providing accessibility to remote areas. It appreciates the operationalization of 
helicopter services in new sectors like Imphal-Ukhrul-Imphal in Manipur. The Committee 
recommends the Ministry to explore the possibility of airstrips in strategic locations as a 
sustainable and reliable alternative to helicopter services for long-term feasibility, economic 
growth and social cohesion in remote regions. The Committee suggests that the Ministry of 
Home Affairs may conduct a survey to identify the abandoned air strips in the North Eastern 
Region which were functional before the independence.  Revival of such air strips may enhance 
connectivity to remote population of the country. 
 (Para 3.6.2) 
INFRASTRUCTURE FOR DISASTER MANAGEMENT 
 The Committee notes the fluctuating capital budget allocation for disaster management 
and the underutilization of allocated funds in both 2023-24 and 2024-25. The Committee 
emphasizes the importance of proactive planning and thorough demand assessment in 
collaboration with the NDMA, NDRF and NIDM to ensure that the budget estimates 
accurately reflect actual needs and demands. To improve fund utilization, the Committee 
recommends streamlining project approval and fund disbursement processes to expedite the 
implementation of planned activities and address any bottlenecks hindering progress. The 
Committee further suggests exploring alternative investments in disaster response 
infrastructure, capacity-building and equipment upgrades to optimize fund usage and enhance 
preparedness. 
(Para 3.7.2) 
CENSUS, SURVEY AND STATISTICS/REGISTRAR GENERAL OF INDIA 
 The Committee notes the progress made in the preparatory work for the Census and 
National Population Register (NPR) and appreciates the continuous efforts in enhancing the 
technological infrastructure and data management systems, particularly the development of 
geospatial databases, the Census Mapping Portal and the revamping of the Civil Registration 
System (CRS) portal. The Committee notes the assurance given by the Ministry that, if 
required, additional funds may be provided at the RE stage for the Census related activities.  
The Committee recommends that the Census may be completed at the earliest. 
 (Para 3.8.5) 
The Committee further appreciates the timely implementation of the amended 
Registration of Births and Deaths Act, 1969 and the successful launch of the revamped CRS 
portal in multiple States/UTs, which will improve accessibility and efficiency in birth and 
death registrations. The automation of the Sample Registration System (SRS) and the 
deployment of improved web portals are commendable steps towards streamlining data 
collection and real-time monitoring. The Committee feels that the Ministry may explore and 
leverage additional existing digital platforms and tools, such as mobile phone connections, 
Aadhaar and other Government data bases for real-time data collection.  Further, linking the 
data collected through these digital channels with the Aadhaar database can provide 

98 
 
additional verification layers, ensuring data integrity and reducing the need for manual cross-
checking.

NATIONAL CYCLONE RISK MITIGATION PROJECT (NCRMP)

DEMAND NO. 51 – POLICE 
 
Central  Armed Police Forces (CAPFs) 
  
The Committee recommends that the Ministry may prioritize the timely utilization of 
allocated funds to avoid any underutilization.  Additionally, the Committee suggests that the 
Ministry need to engage in consultations with CAPFs to assess their long-term capital needs 
and develop a multi-year capital expenditure plan to provide greater certainty and 
predictability in funding.  
 (Para 4.2.4) 
 
Representation of Women in CAPFs 
 
 The Committee observes the Government's positive steps to enhance women's 
representation in CAPFs, including reservations and supportive initiatives like fee exemptions 
and childcare facilities. However, the Committee notes that actual representation remains 
below target. Therefore, it recommends targeted recruitment drives for women, particularly in 
CRPF and CISF. The Committee emphasizes uniform access to support facilities across all 
units and the importance of robust mechanisms to address workplace issues, including a zero 
tolerance policy against sexual harassment. To further improve women's progression, the 
Committee recommends creating mentorship programs and leadership development 
initiatives. It also suggests exploring flexible deployment options or soft postings during 
specific life stages to enhance retention.  
 (Para 4.3.4) 
 
Cadre Review of CAPFs 
 The Committee appreciates the efforts made by the Ministry in conducting Cadre 
Reviews across various CAPFs to enhance promotional avenues and improve operational 
efficiency. It notes that while some forces, such as SSB, CISF, CRPF and ITBP, have benefited 
from recent Cadre Reviews, others, particularly BSF’s Group-B & C (General Duty) Cadre, 
have not been reviewed since its inception in 1965. The Committee emphasizes that Cadre 
Reviews should be undertaken at regular five-year intervals as their effect diminishes over 
time. It observes that timely Cadre Reviews, as seen in recent cases, have significantly 

99 
 
contributed to better workforce planning, increased job satisfaction and improved operational 
effectiveness.  
 (Para 4.4.7) 
 The Committee feels that the MHA along with the Ministry of Finance (MoF) may 
expedite the approval of pending Cadre Reviews, particularly for BSF, Assam Rifles and any 
other forces and to establish a structured mechanism to ensure periodic reviews without 
unnecessary delays. The Committee recommends that financial and structural planning for 
Cadre Reviews be carried out proactively, with dedicated task forces ensuring smooth 
execution. The Committee further recommends that the MHA may explore innovative 
strategies, such as lateral mobility programs, training-based advancements and inter-force 
career progression frameworks, to enhance career growth opportunities across the CAPFs and 
Assam Rifles. 
 (Para 4.4.8) 
Welfare and Rehabilitation Board (WARB) for CAPF 
 The Committee appreciates the initiatives taken by the Ministry for the welfare and 
rehabilitation of CAPF personnel and their families. It notes the financial, educational and 
employment support provided, including ex-gratia payments, scholarships, compassionate 
appointments and employment facilitation through the CAPF Punarvaas initiative. The 
Committee observes that these measures play a crucial role in ensuring the well-being of 
serving and retired personnel, as well as the families of those who have laid down their lives in 
the line of duty. However, the Committee emphasizes the need for greater awareness and 
accessibility of these schemes, ensuring that all eligible beneficiaries receive timely assistance. 
It recommends the Ministry to strengthen the mechanisms and streamline the process of 
disbursing financial benefits, particularly ex-gratia payments and pensions, to minimize 
delays. The Committee also recommends expanding employment opportunities for the 
dependents of deceased personnel by collaborating with more private-sector entities under 
CAPF Punarvaas. Further, the Committee notes the importance of continuous monitoring and 
evaluation of the Bharat Ke Veer Corpus Fund to ensure transparency and effective utilization 
of contributions.  The Committee notes that the ex-gratia lump-sum payment of ₹35 lakhs for 
personnel who die in active duty and ₹25 lakhs for those who die on Government duty.   
 (Para 4.5.5) 
 The Committee further recommends reviewing the current financial allocations to 
welfare schemes, including the 50% GST subsidy on purchases from Kendriya Police Kalyan 
Bhandar, to assess its adequacy and impact. The Committee also emphasizes the importance of 
reserving more seats in professional courses for the children of CAPF personnel and ensuring 
the seamless implementation of the Prime Minister Scholarship Scheme. 
(Para 4.5.6) 
 

100 
 
Housing Facilities and Food Quality for CAPFs 
The Committee recommends that the Ministry may develop a strategic roadmap to 
construct housing units for CAPF personnel in urban or well-connected areas to improve the 
current housing satisfaction level. It emphasizes on utilizing rapid construction technologies to 
expedite the process and ensure timely completion of projects. The Committee recommends 
that the "CAPF e-AWAS" portal be enhanced to improve the allocation of existing 
accommodations by ensuring it serves as an accurate inventory of residential houses and 
Separated Family Accommodation (SFA) across all CAPFs. The Committee wishes to 
emphasize that achieving a housing satisfaction target of 70-80% in the coming years is 
essential for enhancing living conditions for CAPF personnel and their families. 
 (Para 4.6.3) 
The Committee notes with satisfaction the Ministry's explanation of the mess menu 
determination process, highlighting the representative nature of the board responsible for 
menu planning and its adherence to daily caloric intake guidelines. The Committee also 
appreciates the multi-faceted approach regarding food supplies, involving both local Army 
Service Corps (ASC) depots and a designated procurement board. The Committee commends 
the incorporation of millets into the mess menu to enhance nutritional value. Further, the 
Committee acknowledges the importance of regular hygiene inspections by unit doctors and 
the implementation of monthly mess meetings to gather feedback from dining members. The 
Committee recommends the continuation and strengthening of these practices to ensure 
consistently high standards of food quality, hygiene and overall mess arrangements. 
 (Para 4.6.4) 
Manpower in AR & CAPFs 
 The Committee notes the shortfall in posted strength across AR and CAPFs and 
appreciates the Ministry’s ongoing recruitment efforts to fill 45,118 vacant posts. The 
Committee emphasizes the need for expediting the recruitment process to ensure operational 
efficiency and reduce manpower shortages. It urges the Ministry to adopt streamlined 
procedures, enhance training infrastructure and explore fast-track recruitment methods to 
bridge the gap at the earliest. 
(Para 4.7.5) 
 The Committee observes that while there is a considerable representation of SC and ST 
personnel, the number of women personnel remains low. It recommends that the Ministry take 
concrete steps to improve gender representation, such as targeted recruitment drives, better 
incentives and enhanced working conditions for women in CAPFs. The Committee further 
recommends that the Ministry may conduct a periodic workforce assessment to evaluate 
staffing needs and proactively address vacancies. It also suggests improving retention policies, 
welfare measures and career growth opportunities to enhance morale and long-term service 
commitment among personnel. 
 (Para 4.7.6) 

101 
 
K9  Unit/Canine Squad 
The Committee appreciates the vital contributions of K9 units within the CAPF, as they 
provide indispensable support in explosive detection, tracking, anti-Naxal operations and 
counter-terrorism missions. The Committee also commends the rigorous training of these 
canine squads, which enables them to excel in infantry patrol, anti-sabotage sniffer duties and 
detecting bombs and improvised explosive devices (IEDs), making them crucial in high-risk 
operations. The Committee recommends the formation of more K9 squads, considering the 
exceptional services rendered by these units and their potential to further enhance security 
capabilities. 
 (Para 4.8.3)    
Modernization Plan IV for CAPFs 
 The Committee notes the significant financial provisions made for the modernization of 
Central Armed Police Forces (CAPFs) under Modernization Plan IV, with a total budget 
increasing from ₹181.21 crores in BE 2024-25 to ₹352.92 crores in BE 2025-26. The Committee 
emphasizes the need for effective implementation of procurement processes to ensure that 
allocated funds are utilized efficiently and effectively.  It recommends the Ministry to 
streamline procurement procedures to address any bottlenecks that may hinder timely 
acquisition of modern weapons, vehicles and surveillance equipment. Further, the Committee 
suggests that CAPFs should prioritize the procurement of critical items such as modern pistols, 
sniper rifles and advanced surveillance systems to meet their operational requirements. 
 
(Para 4.9.7) 
 
 Further, the Committee recommends that regular monitoring and evaluation 
mechanisms be established to assess the progress of expenditures under the modernization 
plan. This will help identify challenges early on and allow for timely interventions. The 
Committee also recommends for enhanced training programmes for personnel to effectively 
utilize new technologies and equipment acquired through this modernization initiative. 
 
(Para 4.9.8) 
DELHI POLICE 
The Committee notes the efforts made by Delhi Police in implementing new 
technologies and enhancing public services. It appreciates the successful implementation of e-
FIRs and E-NCR facilities, which have significantly improved the ease of reporting crimes. 
The Committee observes that the reduction in capital allocation could impact infrastructure 
development and recommends that efforts be made to enhance capital expenditure to support 
necessary infrastructure improvements. 
 (Para 4.10.6) 
The Committee notes that Delhi Police faces a significant challenge with a sanctioned 
strength of 94,257 personnel but an actual strength of 85,690, resulting in a vacancy of about 
8,567 positions. The Committee urges Delhi Police to expedite the recruitment process to fill 
the existing vacancies. The Committee also appreciates the support provided by deploying 

102 
 
more than ten battalions of CAPFs with the Delhi Police, which to a great extent ameliorate the 
shortage of personnel in Delhi Police. Further, the Committee notes the rising concern of 
crimes against women and suggests more needs to be done to address these crimes effectively. 
The Committee recommends enhancing community awareness through self-defence workshops 
and expanding women-focused programs like Pink Booths and the ‘Himmat’ app. It also 
suggests increasing patrolling in areas deemed unsafe for women and leveraging AI-powered 
surveillance to track suspects in real-time.  
 (Para 4.10.7) 
The Committee recommends providing comprehensive training to police personnel on the use 
of weapons and safety equipment.  
 (Para 4.10.8) 
The Committee recommends that Delhi Police continue to strengthen its forensic 
capabilities and enhance K-9 and Bomb Detection and Disposal Squad (BDT/BDS) units. It 
suggests further integration of advanced technology into policing, such as AI and data 
analytics, to improve crime prevention and detection. Additionally, the Committee notes the 
importance of public engagement and recommends expanding e-services and community 
outreach programs to foster trust and cooperation between the police and the public. 
(Para 4.10.9) 
Traffic Management in Delhi 
4.11.2 The Committee notes the efforts made by Delhi Police in leveraging technology for 
traffic management. It appreciates the implementation of automated challan systems and 
surveillance cameras to monitor traffic violations. The Committee wishes to know how the 
challan collected from the traffic violators are used. The Committee recommends further 
investment in intelligent traffic systems to enhance real-time traffic monitoring and 
management. The Committee further recommends the Delhi Traffic Police to adopt 
international best practices in traffic management, where sensors and AI are used extensively 
to optimize traffic flow. Moreover, the Committee observes the importance of public 
awareness campaigns to promote safe driving practices and recommends collaboration with 
other agencies to develop comprehensive traffic management strategies. 
 (Para 4.11.2) 
SAFE CITY PROJECT 
 The Committee notes the ambitious scope of the Safe City Project and appreciates the 
integration of advanced technologies to enhance safety in Delhi. It recommends Delhi Police to 
ensure timely completion of the project and ensure seamless integration of all technological 
components. The Committee suggests conducting regular public awareness campaigns to 
educate citizens about the benefits and usage of these safety measures. Additionally, it urges 
the police to establish a robust feedback mechanism to continuously improve the project's 
effectiveness based on user experiences and crime data analysis. 
(Para 4.12.2) 

103 
 
 The Committee further recommends that Delhi Police may collaborate with other 
departments to integrate CCTV projects and leverage location-based services to enhance real-
time surveillance and response capabilities. It suggests enhancing community engagement 
through awareness programs focused on gender issues and safety measures, ensuring that the
