(Para 4.12.3) 
CENTRAL  POLICE ORGANIZATIONS 
 
 The Committee notes the significant budgetary allocations for CPOs in the upcoming 
fiscal year, with a total budget of ₹1,754.88 crores for 2025-26. It observes that the budget 
includes both revenue and capital components, with a notable increase in allocations to support 
modernization and operational enhancements across various agencies. The Committee urges 
the Ministry to ensure that these allocations are utilized efficiently, particularly in areas like 
narcotics control and cybercrime, where technological advancements are crucial. For instance, 
the Narcotics Control Bureau (NCB) has been allocated ₹193.77 crores for 2025-26, which 
should be utilized effectively to enhance drug detection capabilities and operationalize new 
field units. Similarly, the Indian Cyber Crime Coordination Centre (I4C) has been allocated 
₹142.81 crores, which should focus on expanding cyber awareness programs and establishing 
specialized units like the Cyber Commandos.The Committee notes that the drug menace is 
affecting the young population of our country in a bad way.  The Ministry of Home Affairs 
should take more steps to address the issue in association with NCB. 
(Para 4.13.4) 
 The Committee recommends the NCRB to effectively utilize its increased budget for 
2025-26, to enhance its data analysis capabilities. It emphasizes the importance of leveraging 
advanced technologies, such as artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning, to improve the 
depth and accuracy of crime statistics. The Committee urges NCRB to ensure that all crime 
data is published regularly and made accessible to law enforcement agencies and public. It also 
notes the publication of agitation data by the NCRB and suggests continuing of this 
publication. The Committee further recommends that NCRB to collaborate with international 
agencies, to adopt best practices in crime data collection and analysis.  Additionally, it suggests 
that the Directorate of Coordination and Police Wireless (DCPW) may focus on upgrading 
communication systems to ensure seamless coordination among law enforcement agencies. The 
Committee emphasizes that each CPO should provide regular updates on expenditure and 
project implementation to ensure that the allocated funds are used effectively to enhance 
public safety and security. The Committee notes that cybercrime in the country has increased 
manifold. A notable characteristic of cybercrimes is that they often involve multiple State and 
police jurisdictions.  Therefore, it is essential that the Police authorities must get the power of 
seamless jurisdiction to deal with the Cyber Crimes.  The Committee recommends that a 
separate Central Police Force may be established on the lines of NIA or CBI to deal with Cyber 
Crimes. 
 (Para 4.13.5) 
 
 
 
 

104 
 
FORENSIC SCIENCES FACILITIES AND INFRASTRUCTURE 
 
 The Committee notes the significant efforts to enhance forensic capabilities, 
particularly through the strengthening of DNA and cyber forensic facilities under the 
Nirbhaya Fund. It recommends the Ministry to continue to invest in modernizing forensic 
infrastructure, focusing on advanced technologies to improve analysis efficiency. The 
Committee suggests the Ministry to ensure that all forensic laboratories, both central and 
State, are equipped with the latest equipment and trained personnel to handle complex cases 
effectively. It emphasizes the importance of maintaining a centralized database of forensic data 
to aid in investigations and policy-making. Additionally, it suggests that the Ministry to 
consider establishing more regional forensic laboratories to increase accessibility and reduce 
the burden on existing facilities. The Committee notes that new Criminal Laws mandates 
forensic investigation for offences involving punishment of 7 years or more.  The Committee 
also notes that there are very few forensic science laboratories.  The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that every district of the country must have a forensic laboratory to deal with the 
criminal investigation. The Committee is aware that criminal investigation is a State subject 
but given the financial health of the States, the Union Government should financially support 
the establishment of forensic laboratories in each district of the country. 
 (Para 4.14.6) 
The Committee also recommends that the Ministry needs to prioritize the establishment 
of off-campuses of the National Forensic Sciences University (NFSU) and Centers of Excellence 
to provide quality training and education in forensic sciences. It suggests that mobile forensic 
vans be deployed to enhance on-site forensic capabilities, particularly in remote areas. 
 (Para 4.14.7) 
BORDER INFRASTRUCTURE AND MANAGEMENT 
 
The Committee notes the significant efforts of the Ministry to enhance border 
infrastructure across various international borders. The Committee recommends that the 
Government needs to expedite the completion of ongoing fencing projects, ensuring that all 
allocated funds are utilized efficiently. It also recommends conducting regular assessments to 
identify gaps in border infrastructure and ensure that allocations are aligned with strategic 
priorities. The Committee also wishes the Ministry to prepare the data of influx of immigrants 
like Bangladeshis, Rohingyas and from other countries. The Committee notes that Rohingyas 
are entering and settling illegally in various parts of the country. The Committee recommends 
that the Ministry of Home Affairs should take effective steps to identify the illegally settled 
Rohingyas and repatriate them to their parent countries. 
 (Para 4.15.6) 
 
The Committee suggests that the Ministry needs to consider establishing more 
integrated check posts to facilitate smoother trade and travel while maintaining security 
standards. The Committee recommends that the MHA needs to continue to enhance border 
security through the expansion of smart fencing projects, which are part of the Comprehensive 
Integrated Border Management System (CIBMS). These initiatives should use advanced 
technologies such as thermal imagers, fiber-optic sensors, radar and sonar systems to create an 

105 
 
invisible electronic barrier that aids in real-time surveillance and intrusion detection, thereby 
strengthening India's border security. 
(Para 4.15.7) 
 
SCHEMES FOR THE SAFETY OFWOMEN 
 
 The Committee acknowledges and commends the Ministry's substantial efforts to 
enhance women's safety through various schemes under the Nirbhaya Fund. Initiatives such as 
the Emergency Response Support System (ERSS), Safe City Projects and the strengthening of 
Forensic Science Labs reflect a holistic approach to combat violence against women. The 
Committee observes that the allocation for ERSS Phase II in 2025-26 will focus on upgrading 
the existing system to ensure a standardized and efficient emergency response mechanism 
nationwide.  However, the Committee notes the underutilization of funds allocated under these 
schemes. The Committee recommends the Ministry to develop a centralized digital dashboard 
to track real-time progress of projects under the Nirbhaya Fund. Further, a mid-term review 
mechanism should be institutionalized to identify underperforming projects and reallocate 
unspent funds to high-impact initiatives. 
 
 (Para 4.16.5) 
The Committee recommends that the Ministry needs to expand training programs for 
investigating officers, prosecutors and medical officers to improve the handling of forensic 
evidence and sexual assault cases. It emphasizes the need for increased awareness and 
capacity-building initiatives to address violence against women effectively. The Committee also 
suggests the MHA to consider integrating more technology-driven solutions, such as AI-
powered surveillance systems and mobile apps, to enhance women's safety in public spaces.  
 
(Para 4.16.6) 
INTER-OPERABLECRIMINALJUSTICESYSTEM (ICJS) 
 
 The Committee notes and appreciates the significant efforts by the Ministry to enhance 
the criminal justice system through the Inter-Operable Criminal Justice System (ICJS). This 
initiative has the potential to revolutionize the way data is shared and utilized across different 
pillars of the justice system. The Committee recommends that MHA needs to prioritize the 
effective implementation of ICJS across all States and Union territories by ensuring timely 
allocation and utilization of funds. It suggests that the Ministry of Home Affairs work closely 
with the National Crime Records Bureau (NCRB) and the National Informatics Centre (NIC) 
to ensure that the cloud-based infrastructure is secure and scalable. The Committee further 
recommends that necessary capacity building and training programs for officials involved in 
the ICJS needs to be given to ensure they are equipped to handle the advanced technologies 
and data management systems effectively.  
 (Para 4.17.4) 
MODERNIZATION OF PRISONS  
 The Committee appreciates the Ministry for its ongoing efforts in the modernization of 
prisons, which focus on enhancing security and promoting rehabilitation through technological 
advancements, infrastructure improvements and effective correctional administration. 

106 
 
However, considering the funding gap caused by the transition to the CNA TSA Hybrid Model 
1A, the Committee emphasizes the need for a smooth transition and calls on the Ministry to 
proactively address challenges to ensure the timely disbursement of funds. 
 (Para 4.18.7) 
 
The Committee appreciates the Ministry's adherence to the Model Prison Manual, 2016 
and the Model Prisons & Correctional Services Act in ensuring the separation of undertrials 
and convicted prisoners. It emphasizes the necessity for full implementation of those guidelines 
by States and UTs, even though the prison administration falls under the State List. The 
Ministry’s efforts to address undertrials’ detention through Section 479 of the 
BharatiyaNagarik Suraksha Sanhita, 2023 (BNSS) and the Special Campaign launched on 
Constitution Day are acknowledged by the Committee. The Committee suggests continued 
monitoring and collaboration with States and UTs to ensure the timely release of eligible 
undertrials and the submission of monthly progress reports.  
 
(Para 4.18.8) 
 
 The Committee recommends that there may be separate centers of detention for 
undertrials. It should not be referred to as jails, instead they may be called correction centers 
or detention centers. Jails should be used only to accommodate the hardened convicted 
criminals.  This may go a long way in preventing the undertrials becoming hardened criminals, 
providing more scope for reforming the undertrials and escaping the stigma of jailed 
prisoners. 
 (Para 4.18.9) 
Further, the Committee recommends the allocation of dedicated funds for capital 
expenditure alongside revenue expenditure to facilitate substantial and long-term 
improvements in prison infrastructure. It also recognizes the significant role of skill 
development and rehabilitation programs for prisoners and encourages the Ministry to ensure 
these initiatives receive sufficient funding and are effectively implemented in all States and 
Union Territories. 
(Para 4.18.10) 
The Committee notes that the Ministry has issued guidelines to the State and UTs 
through the Model Prison Manual and the Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act. The 
Committee recommends that these guidelines be fully implemented across all jurisdictions to 
address mobile phone usage in prisons. It also suggests enhancing the penalties prescribed in 
the Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act for using contraband in prisons.  
(Para 4.18.11) 
Support to Poor Prisoners 
 
 The Committee notes and appreciates the Government's efforts to support poor 
prisoners through this scheme. It observes that the slow pace of fund utilization by States and 

107 
 
Union Territories is a significant concern. By effectively implementing this scheme, the 
Committee believes that it will help reduce the overcrowding of prisons by facilitating the 
release of prisoners who are unable to afford bail or fines. This will also contribute to reducing 
the number of undertrial prisoners, thereby decongesting prisons and ensuring that justice is 
delivered more efficiently. The Committee recommends that the Ministry to review the 
implementation of this scheme to identify barriers to fund utilization and ensure that funds are 
effectively used to benefit the intended beneficiaries. It suggests revising the eligibility criteria 
to include prisoners involved in multiple cases, ensuring broader coverage of the scheme. 
 
(Para 4.19.3) 
Land Port Authority of India (LPAI) 
The Committee emphasizes the need for enhanced infrastructure development at 
Integrated Check Posts (ICPs) to improve cross-border trade efficiency. It notes that the 
increase in operational ICPs from 12 to 15 in FY 2024-25 is a positive step but suggests that 
more focus should be placed on completing ongoing projects efficiently. The Committee 
highlights the importance of timely completion of land acquisition and pre-construction 
activities for new ICPs. It emphasizes the need for swift approval processes to expedite project 
initiation. The Committee recommends prioritizing the completion of construction work at 
existing projects, such as the Land Ports at Sunauli, Sutarkandi, and Bhithamore, to ensure 
operationalization and efficiency in cross-border trade. 
(Para 4.20.2) 
The Committee emphasizes the need for robust infrastructure at all ICPs to enhance the 
efficiency of customs clearance and trade facilitation processes. It suggests that LPAI should 
engage in more extensive stakeholder consultations to identify and address common challenges 
faced by Land Customs Stations (LCPs) and ICPs, such as limited storage facilities and 
insufficient amenities. The Committee recommends conducting regular assessments to evaluate 
the efficiency of clearance processes at ICPs and identify areas for improvement. 
 (Para 4.20.3) 
MODERNIZATION OF POLICE FORCES (MoPF) 
 The Committee notes the significant efforts by the MHA to modernize police forces 
across India through the "Modernization of Police Forces" scheme. The Committee 
recommends that the Government has to prioritize the effective utilization of funds under the 
"Assistance to States for Modernization of Police" scheme. It suggests that States and Union 
territories be encouraged to reduce unspent balances by ensuring timely implementation of 
projects. The Committee feels that the Ministry has to enhance collaboration with State 
Governments to address procedural delays and inefficiencies in the implementation of the 
Modernization of Police Forces scheme. Additionally, it recommends that the Ministry may 
explore international best practices in police modernization to enhance effectiveness and 
improve public safety. 
 
(Para 4.21.7) 

108 
 
 The Committee appreciates the Government's efforts in substantial reduction in Left 
Wing Extremism activities and recommends continued support for these initiatives to further 
reduce LWE influence and improve security and development in affected areas. Further, the 
Committee suggests that the Government may consider enhancing security in LWE areas by 
improving the capabilities of Special Forces and local police. 
 
(Para 4.21.8) 
DEMAND NO. 52 TO 59 - UNION TERRITORIES 
 
 The Committee notes, from the perusal of the overall budgetary provisions and 
expenditure provided by the Ministry that most of the Union Territories have performed well 
with regard to the utilization of funds against allocation made in RE 2024-25. Although the 
funds utilization in Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu and Lakshadweep are 
comparatively low, still they have been able to utilize more that 70% of the funds against 
allocation made in RE 2024-25. The Committee appreciates the efforts of the Union Territories 
to efficiently utilize their allocated funds.  
 
 (Para 5.1.3) 
 
The Committee feels that people’s representatives in a democratic setup are well 
acquainted with the priorities, needs and aspirations of the people of their constituency. They 
can play an important role in representing the needs of the local population of their 
constituencies to the UT Administration. The Administration of UTs can seek participation of 
the people’s representatives of the Union Territories while planning and implementation of 
developmental projects of the UTs. 
 
(Para 5.1.4) 
 
Demand No.52-Andaman and Nicobar Islands 
 
The Committee notes that water supply, power, medical and public health, roads and 
bridges are some of the schemes where allocation have been enhanced for the Financial Year 
2025-26 as compared to the Financial Year 2024-25. The Committee is of the view that these 
schemes are very important for the infrastructural development of the UT and making life 
easier for the local population. The Committee observes that allocation of budget is the basic 
step towards implementation of a scheme, but without regular and periodic monitoring of the 
projects status, the budget can remain underutilized. The Committee, therefore, recommends 
that the UT administration should ensure implementation of the schemes/projects included in 
the BE 2025-26 and the utilization of entire allocation within the Financial year. 
 
(Para 5.2.3) 
 
The Committee notes that a new Integrated Terminal Building (NITB) at Veer 
Savarkar International Airport, Sri Vijaya Puramhas been made operational since August 
2023 and the annual passenger handling capacity of the airport has increased from 0.75 
Million Passengers Per Annum (MPPA) to 5 million MPPA. The Committee feels that 
commencement of international flights from A&N Islands must have also given a boost to the tourism sector by attracting more number of foreign tourists. The Committee would 

109 
 
like to be apprised about the impact of development in tourism sector on revenue generation in the UT and on socio economic growth of the local people of the A&N Islands. The benefits of economic growth in the UT must also percolate to the local population as they equally deserve to reap those benefits. The Committee also feels that 
there are a number of islands in the UT of Andaman and Nicobar Islands which are not 
inhabited but have beautiful beaches and potential for eco tourism. The Committee 
recommends that UT administration may explore the opportunity of development of the 
tourism related activities in these islands and develop them as tourist destination for the 
growing number of tourists in the UT. 
 
(Para 5.2.7) 
 
The Committee notes that renewable energy sources constituted only 10.35 % of total 
power generation during FY 2023-24, which is very low. The Committee should be informed 
about the outcome of feasibility study for Solar & Wind energy which has been conducted by 
The Energy and Resources Institute (TERI) and National Institute of Wind Energy (NIWE) 
respectively to harness Renewable Energy. The Committee, therefore, recommends that 
Ministry of Home Affairs along with the UT administration may approach the concerned 
Ministry/ Department to bring the new proposals of producing electricity through renewable 
energy resources at the earliest, to reduce the dependence on non-renewable sources of energy 
and preserve the fragile island from the negative impact of climate change. 
 
(Para 5.2.10) 
 
 The Committee notes that the various projects and schemes to be initiated in the UT of 
Andaman & Nicobar Islands in Financial Year 2025-26 under the Forestry and Wildlife Sector 
are related to construction works. There are not many activities relating to wildlife 
conservation. The UT has very rich wildlife as well as marine life. Giant leatherback Sea turtle 
is one of the species which is facing the threat of extinction in the UT. The Committee feels that 
enough hatcheries should be maintained to protect eggs of sea turtles. Efforts must also be 
made to have regular coordination and collaboration with concerned organizations for 
protecting natural habitat and nesting grounds of sea turtles. 
 
(Para 5.2.12) 
  
The Committee notes that the allocation of ₹503.92 crores has been made in BE 2025-26 
for Shipping and it is proposed to be utilized for the construction of High Speed Emergency 
response Vessel., chartering of passenger vessels, procurements of boats and maintenance and 
management and of vessels. The Committee also notes that during the Financial Year 2024-25 
(upto 31.12.2024), an expenditure of ₹239 crores has been incurred on the payment released to 
SCI towards operation and maintenance management of Mainland, Inter Island and foreshore 
vessels. The Committee is of the view that a major portion of budgetary allocation is used for 
the operation and maintenance management of vessels so enough funds could not be available 
for the procurement of new vessels. The Committee feels traveling through waterways is one of 
the major forms of connectivity within Islands of the UT. The Committee, therefore, 
recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs along with UT administration must work out 
plans with concerned Ministries/Departments to procure small passenger vessels for enhancing 

110 
 
the waterways connectivity within the islands of UT and seek additional funds from the 
Ministry of Finance for the same.  
 
(Para 5.2.16) 
 
The Committee appreciates the action of the Government agencies which were involved 
in the geographical survey of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands which revealed the existence 
of 836 islands in place of the hitherto 572 islands in the UT of A&NI and recommends that full 
potential of these islands may be explored for various economic and tourism related activities.   
 
(Para 5.2.17) 
Demand No. 53 –Chandigarh 
 
 The Committee notes that that the UT of Chandigarh had projected a demand of 
₹7924.58 crores (Gross basis), but the Government of India has allocated ₹6983.18 crores 
(Gross basis) for the Financial Year 2025-26. There is a shortfall of ₹941.40 Cr. (Gross and Net 
Basis) as compared to the allocations made and demand projected. The Committee feels that 
such substantial shortfall in allocation can hamper the progress of ongoing infrastructure 
projects in the field of health, urban development and education as envisaged by the UT 
administration for the upcoming Financial Year. The commencement of new projects could 
also be affected by the shortage of funds as focus will be on completion of ongoing projects 
rather than initiating any new schemes. The Committee, therefore, recommends that efforts 
must be made by the UT administration to strictly monitor the progress of projects and 
utilization of funds, so that it can seek additional fund at RE stage as per revised requirement.   
 
 (Para 5.3.6) 
 
 The Committee notes that UT administration of Chandigarh has earmarked₹76.60 
crores for the infrastructure development in technical and higher education sector and 
Government schools for the Financial Year 2025-26. The Committee recommends that efforts 
must be initiated by the concerned agencies to adhere to the completion of 
construction/extension of the proposed buildings at the earliest.  
(Para 5.3.7) 
 
The Committee is of the view that in any school availability of fundamental facilities is 
paramount to fostering a conducive learning environment. These facilities not only ensure the 
safety and well-being of students but also contribute significantly to their academic growth and 
overall development. Improvement of infrastructure should be topmost priority for the 
education sector. The Committee recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs may provide 
sufficient funds to the UT administration at RE stage for the education sector for improvement 
of basic facilities like sufficient number of  classrooms, toilets, libraries, laboratories and sports 
facilities at city Government schools. Measures should also be taken towards creation of smart 
classrooms by digitizing the existing ones and provision of nutritious food for mid day meals.   
 
(Para 5.3.8) 
 The Committee was informed that the teachers and parents are slightly agitated over 
some issues related to school education in the Union Territory of Chandigarh. The Committee 

111 
 
recommends that the Administration may consult with all the stakeholders involved and 
address their issues they are facing in a positive way.  
(Para 5.3.9) 
 
The Committee notes that the UT administration of Chandigarh has been incurring 
huge expenditure on account of purchasing electricity. The Committee recommends that the 
Administration of Chandigarh should take measures to focus on renewable energy sources to 
reduce the demand for purchasing of power from various agencies. Further, energy 
production through renewable energy resources can be a step in the direction of green and 
clean smart city.  
 (Para  5.3.13) 
DemandNo.54- Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu 
The Committee notes that an allocation of ₹ 86.00 crores and ₹ 20.00 crores have been 
earmarked for Infrastructure Project under Silvassa Smart City Limited Phase – 2 and for the 
construction of Shri Vinoba Bhave Civil Hospital and Allied Building (Phase-II) at Dadra 
&Nagar Haveli, Silvassa respectively for the Financial Year 2025-26. The Committee feels that 
the both the projects are pivotal for the infrastructural development of the UT.  Further, the 
establishment of Shri Vinoba Bhave Civil Hospital can cater to the advanced healthcare needs 
of the population of the UT. The Committee recommends that the UT Administration of Dadra 
and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu should ensure that the progress of these projects must 
be regularly monitored and budget allocated for these projects are fully utilized during the 
fiscal year so that timely completion of the already approved and ongoing projects can be 
achieved within the prescribed time frame. 
(Para 5.4.4) 
 
The Committee observes that out of the allocation of ₹ 56.31 crores in the FY 2025-2026 
under the head “Transmission and Distribution of Power”, ₹40.01 crores will be utilized 
mainly for establishment of Sub-Station, replacement of old transformer; and supply, erection, 
testing and commissioning of Power transformers. Further, during the year 2024-25 most of 
the projects were related to maintenance of existing power infrastructure. Energy 
consumption is one of the most important indices that decide the development of a region. 
Access to electricity round the clock in each and every household is a major step in this 
direction. It would lead to the rapid economic development of the UT.  The Committee would 
like to be apprised about the round the clock availability of electricity in the UT and the 
measures that are taken by the UT Administration towards the tapping the renewable energy 
resources to meet the demands of power generation.  
(Para 5.4.7) 
 
 The Committee is of the view that UT of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu 
has scenic and serene beauty. Its pristine beaches, colonial-era architecture and unique blend 
of Indian and Portuguese culture has the potential to attract tourists like other coastal Union 
Territories, if given enough promotion and publicity. The Committee recommends that 
measures should be taken by the UT administration of Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman 
and Diu to promote organizing regional festivals, showcasing cultural heritage through such 
festivals, events showcasing traditional arts and crafts, creation of homestay facilities, etc. 
Further, exploring the scope of adventure activities and promoting adventure tour operators 
can also give impetus to tourism sector in the UT. These initiatives will not only boost tourism 

112 
 
in the UT but can contribute towards creation of local employment opportunities in UT. 
 
(Para 5.4.10) 
 
The Committee observes that for the Schemes under Medical and Health, the UT of 
Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu has been able to utilize ₹185.92 crores during the 
FY 2024-25 till 31.12.2024 and some of the projects under medical and health sectors have 
more than 80% of completion status. The Committee recommends that the administration 
must keep striving for timely completion of the health related projects as it is very essential to 
avoid cost-overruns in future.  The Committee recommends that apart from development of 
health infrastructure, the UT administration should also ensure the recruitment of sufficient 
number of health care workers and professionals in the various hospitals, health and wellness 
centres and primary health centres in the UT as availability of health-care professionals in 
these hospital/centres can provide immediate health requirements to the local population of the 
UT. 
(Para 5.4.14) 
Demand No. 55 –Ladakh 
 
The Committee observes that the fund allocations for the Union Territory of Ladakh 
has been reduced for the Financial Year 2025-26 to ₹ 4692.15 crores (Net basis) as compared to 
allocation of ₹5958.00 crores in the Financial Year 2024-25. The Committee feels that the 
reduction in budgetary allocation can hamper the ongoing works and schemes of the Union 
Territory. The Committee recommends that the Ministry of Home affairs may need to pursue 
the additional fund requirement with Ministry of Finance at RE stage if the UT of Ladakh 
seeks additional funds at later stage.  
(Para 5.5.5) 
 The Union Territory of Ladakh has immense power potential in the solar and wind 
energy sector due to its high altitude and bright sky and dry weather condition. The 
Committee appreciates that the UT administration has taken up various projects to harness 
the renewable resources of energy. The Committee would like to be apprised about the 
percentage of power generated from the solar energy sector and initiatives taken by the UT 
Administration to generate power from wind resource.   
 
(Para 5.5.6) 
 
The Committee notes the steps taken by the UT administration of Ladakh for 
promoting employment opportunities for local youth in the region. Providing end to end 
support to the artisans and crafts persons through subsidized loans, tools and equipment for 
expanding and building their businesses through the PM Vishwakarma scheme is one such 
scheme which can give boost to self sustainable small scale entrepreneurship in the UT. 
Ladakh is a high yielding pashmina and wool grower district and it involved various crafts 
like weaving, spinning, dying, tailoring and knitting. UT administration must ensure that 
enough financial as well as administrative and skill development support must be provided to 
the local artisans and craftsmen for the preservation of the traditional craftsmanship of 
Ladakh and upliftment of artisans and weavers of Ladakh.  
 
(Para 5.5.9) 

113 
 
 
 The Committee notes that there are 1275 vacancies in Gazetted posts and 3596 
vacancies in non-Gazetted posts in various departments of the UT of Ladakh. Although steps 
have been taken by the UT administration to fill the vacant posts, the Committee feels number 
of vacancies are huge in the UT and shortage of officers and staff in various UT administration 
can affect the smooth and seamless functioning of theadministration. The Committee 
recommends that the process of recruitment to fill the vacant posts must be sped up so that the 
educated young generation of the UT should not be bereft of employment opportunities in the 
UT itself.   
 
(Para 5.5.11) 
 
 The Committee appreciates the efforts taken by theUT Administration to augment the 
drinking water supply to the Leh and Kargil towns under 24x7 Water Supply Scheme  
andproviding potable drinking water facility through Functional Household Tap Connections 
(FHTCs) to the rural households. The Committee recommends that the Government may 
extend necessary funds and resources to cover the remaining rural areas as well, at the earliest, 
to make potable water available to all the households. 
 
(Para 5.5.15) 
 
 The Committee recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs along with UT 
administration of Ladakh must pursue with the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs 
(MoHUA), Govt. of India for setting up of 11 MLD plant for which Detailed Project Reports 
(DPRs) have been submitted at an estimated cost of ₹390.00 crores for Leh town. 
 
 (Para 5.5.16) 
 
 The Committee notes the various steps taken for solid waste management in Ladakh 
like implementation of a comprehensive door-to-door waste collection system across Leh and 
Kargil Towns and construction of Solid Waste Management Plant in Kargil with a capacity of 
25 TPD (Tonnes per Day) and is of the view that the UT administration is doing commendable 
job in segregation and management of waste disposal. The Committee recommends that the 
UT Administration must ensure that the comprehensive ban on single-use plastics in Leh and 
Kargil towns must be strictly adhere to which will help in reducing land and water pollution in 
Ladakh. 
 
(Para 5.5.19) 
 
 The Committee was informed that the UT administration is giving all out support to the 
people to give maximum health care facilities. Last year the mother mortality rate was zero in 
the UT of Ladakh mainly because the pregnant mothers are admitted to the hospitals for 
delivery and deliveries are taking place in the hospitals. The Committee appreciates the 
achievement of the UT administration in this regard. The Committee suggests and 
recommends that the experiences and practices adopted by the UT of Ladakh in the health 
sector can be followed in other UTs and States also. 
 
(Para 5.5.21) 

114 
 
 
Demand No. 56–Lakshadweep 
 The Committee recommends that efforts must be made by the UT administration to 
strictly monitor the progress of projects and utilization of funds, so that it can seek additional 
fund at RE stage as per revised requirements.   
(Para 5.6.4) 
 
 The Committee notes that the Lakshadweep Administration has taken various 
measures to tap renewable energy in consultation with the Solar Energy Corporation of India 
(SECI).  The Committee feels that after the Commission of 1.7 MW Solar Plant with 1.4 MW 
Battery Energy Storage System (BESS) Kavaratti and Agatti island, the UT administration 
will be able to reduce the diesel consumption and save huge amount of  funds. At the same time 
harnessing power from solar plants is a pivotal shift from diesel based power to a sustainable, 
eco-friendly energy source for the region as power generation through solar plants will reduce 
carbon emissions. The Committee would like to be apprised about the savings which may 
occur on account of the commissioning of this solar plant and the details of the projects on 
which the UT administration of Lakshadweep plans to utilize these funds.  
(Para 5.6.6) 
 
 The Committee notes that the UT of Lakshadweep is focusing on land 
acquisition/construction of buildings for Hospitals, Nursing College & Para Medical College 
and maintenance of existing buildings in health sector and land & buildings for schools, 
Colleges& Polytechnic and its maintenance in education sector for the development of these 
sectors in the UT. The Committee also notes that during the Financial Year 2024-25, the UT 
administration has been able to spend 39.02% and 64.13% of RE allocation in schemes 
relating to health and education, sports, arts and culture respectively, till 31.12.2024, which is 
very low. Therefore, the Committee recommends that the UT administration should take 
proactive and diligent steps to improve the fund utilization pattern of these sectors in ensuing 
financial year so that the projects under these schemes do not get suffered due to 
underutilization of funds.  
 
(Para 5.6.10) 
 
The Committee observes that huge allocation of funds have been sought during the RE 
2024-25 mainly for clearing the pending liabilities to Shipping Corporation of India (SCI), 
Lakshadweep Development Corporation Limited (LDCL) and Cochin Shipyard Limited (CSL) 
towards the operations, repair and maintenance of Ships. The Committee notes that out of 5 
all-weather passenger-cum-cargo ships, only one is functional.  The Committee recommends 
that all the four ships which are under repair may be made operational at the earliest to 
alleviate the transportation problems of Lakshadweep. The Committee also takes note of other 
issues pertaining to vacant posts and non-functional desalination plant in Kavaratti island. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry of Home Affairs may convene a meeting 
with the Administration of the UT of  Lakshadweep to discuss and find an amicable solution to 
these issues pertaining to the UT of Lakshadweep. 
 
(Para 5.6.15) 
 

115 
 
Demand No. 57 –  Transfers to Delhi 
 
5.7.4 The Committee recommends that the Ministry must ensure that regular and periodic 
monitoring of ongoing and new schemes/projects proposed under Central Assistance to UTs 
for which an allocation ₹951.00 crores has been made to the UT of Delhi by the Union 
Government, so that these projects can fully utilize the fund allocation and avoid cost overruns 
and strictly adhere to their completion time line.  
 
(Para 5.7.4) 
  
The Committee notes that Chandrawal Water Treatment Plant project is proposed to 
be executed in six packages and expected to be completed by September, 2027. Earlier, the 
project was expected to be completed in October, 2024. This shows that the completion of the 
project has been delayed. Considering the shortage of water in different parts of Delhi, the 
Committee observes that the project is vital for Delhi as it will help in improving water supply 
system for population of 22 lakh i.e. about 11% of Delhi population. Therefore, the Committee 
recommends that the MHA may take up with the UT of Delhi for periodic monitoring of the 
progress of work so that the timelines are met without cost overruns. Adequate funds may be 
sought from the Ministry of Finance as per the requirement.  
(Para 5.7.8) 
 
 
The Committee is of view that a substantial number of private coaching centres and 
hostels for boys and girls are being run in Delhi without any strict norms and regulations. 
These are not providing adequate space and basic living conditions to the young population 
who come from far-flung areas for fulfilling their future employment aspirations.  The 
Committee would like to be apprised about the number of such coaching centres and hostels 
that are being run in Delhi and what kind to accommodation and other basic facilities are 
being provided by them to the students and residents of these facilities. The Committee may 
also be informed about the provision of licensing of such centres and the last inspection done to 
review the conditions of these centres and hostels.  
(Para 5.7.10) 
 
  
The Committee also like to be informed about the maintenance of any kind of record of 
around 25-30 Lakh floating population in the region of Delhi and what kind of basic facilities 
are available for them. 
(Para 5.7.11) 
 
 
Demand No. 58 - Transfers to Jammu & Kashmir 
 
 The Committee notes that the UT administration of Jammu and Kashmir has achieved 
successful and nearing completion of various projects in the sectors of road, rail and air 
connectivity, power generation and provision of drinking water in rural households. The 
Committee feels empowerment in infrastructure projects creates conducive environment for 
investments in industries and create opportunities for entrepreneurship in underdeveloped 
regions. The Committee recommends that UT Administration should take measures to 

116 
monitor the progress of ongoing projects in these sectors and speed up the projects which are 
in pipeline.  
(Para 5.8.6) 
The Committee is heartened to note that the tourists’ arrival in J&K for the last two 
years have increased manifold. The Committee takes notes of the steps taken for the 
development and promotion of tourism in the UT of Jammu and Kashmir. The Committee is 
of the view that the tourism and hospitality sector, apart from generating large revenue, is a 
major source of livelihood for the people of J&K by creating employment opportunities and 
boosting local businesses. It also contributes towards regional and rural development of the 
UT. The Committee recommends that UT administration should chalk out a road map to take 
initiatives to position J&K as a world class tourism destination, improve the tourist experience 
and create sustainable economic opportunities for the local population.  
(Para 5.8.10) 
The Committee is of the view that the industrial sector in Jammu & Kashmir needs to 
be enhanced for generation of employment for the local youth. The Committee recommends 
that initiatives should be taken to improve infrastructure i.e. investing in transportation, 
power supply, and internet connectivity to make region more attractive for businesses and 
investors.  At the same time, by encouraging entrepreneurship, promoting tourism, enhancing 
education and skill development, the employment potential of Jammu and Kashmir can be 
significantly increased. Providing loans on subsidised rates can also improve employment 
scenario in the UT.  
(Para 5.8.18) 
The Committee recommends that the Administration of Jammu and Kashmir should 
take more concrete steps towards providing employment opportunities to educated youth of 
the UT. Additional initiatives should be taken for implementation of self-employment schemes 
across various sectors to establish sustainable income generating ventures for unemployed 
youth.  
(Para 5.8.19) 
Handicraft sector in the UT of Jammu and Kashmir is one of the largest employment 
generation sources in the region. The UT administration should strive for Geographical 
Indication (GI) tagging of each and every crafts of the region as it can  promote cultural 
heritage and economic growth as with GI tagged product, the producers are able to get 
significantly better prices for their products.  
(Para 5.8.20) 
Demand No. 59- Transfers to Puducherry 
The Committee notes that 98.17% of funds have been utilized by the UT of Puducherry 
(till 31 stJanuary, 2025) in the FY 2024-25, but the allocation at BE stage in FY 2025-26 has 
been reduced to ₹3427.18 crores against the projected amount of ₹3766.33 crores, under 

117 
“Central Assistance”. The shortfall in allocation for programme/activities under this head for  
the Financial Year 2025-26 should be reviewed vis-à-vis actual progress of implementation and 
if required additional fund must be sought at RE 2025-26 stage. The Committee, recommends 
that in future, the Ministry of Home Affairs should impress upon the Ministry of Finance to 
increase the allocation of funds in accordance with the projected demand of the Government of 
Puducherry as the UT has been a maintaining a very healthy utilization pattern.  
(Para 5.9.5) 
The Committee notes that the Airport Authority of India has approved the plan for the 
expansion of Puducherry Airport, and finalized a Master Plan. The Committee recommends 
that the MHA may take up with the UT of Puducherry, concerned Ministries/ Departments, to 
expedite the further necessary activities in this direction and made efforts to provide requisite 
funds for the plan. The Committee would like to be apprised about the progress made in this 
regard. 
(Para 5.9.6) 
The Committee appreciates that the UT administration is working towards developing 
Puducherry into a sustainable tourism destination. The Committee is of the opinion that 
Puducherry has multiple tourist attractions and some unique identity owing to history of 
French influence. Tourism has a potential to become a major source of employment and 
income/revenue generation with active participation of the private sector and local host 
communities.  The UT should take steps to fully develop the tourism potential of UT of 
Puducherry in a planned and integrated manner and to augment the infrastructure facilities 
for tourists and promote socio economic development through tourism while preserving and 
protecting the environment and heritage. 
(Para 5.9.10)

2. At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members to the meeting of the
Committee. He informed the Members about the agenda of the meeting which
includes the presentation of Home Secretary on the Demands for Grants (2025-26)

122
of the Ministry of Home Affairs pertaining to the Ministry, Central Armed Police 
Forces and Central Police Organizations, followed by discussion on the said 
subject.   
(On arrival of witnesses) 
3. The Chairman welcomed the Home Secretary and Senior Officers of the
Ministry of Home Affairs to the meeting of the Committee, initiated the discussion
on the Demands for Grants of the Ministry of Home Affairs for the year 2025-26.
4. The Home Secretary briefed the Committee about the responsibilities of the
Ministry which include internal security, border management, Central-State
relations, administration of Union Territories, management of Central Armed
Police Forces (CAPFs), Disaster management, to counter left-wing extremism,
cyber crime, etc. and initiatives taken by the Ministry to discharge its
responsibilities. He apprised the Committee about the Demands for Grants (2025-
26) of the Ministry of Home Affairs under Demand Nos. (49-59) including Central
Armed Police Forces (CAPFs), Central Police Organizations (CPOs), Delhi Police
and the Union Territories.
5. The Ministry of Home Affairs made a detailed presentation before the
Committee that inter-alia included Budget allocation (under Demand No.49) made
under the various schemes like relief & rehabilitation for migrants and repatriates;
Freedom Fighter (pension & other benefits); Helicopter Services for NE Region,
Ladakh, J&K and HP; infrastructure for disaster management; establishment of
Bhartiya Bhasha Anubhag; policies and guidelines regarding Disaster
Management; mitigation projects approved under NDMF (National Disaster
Mitigation Fund); steps taken to strengthen National Disaster Relief Force
(NDRF); major achievements in Official Language like integration of memory
based translation software 'kanthastha' into the e-office of various
Ministries/Department of Government of India, release of second edition of “Hindi
Shabd Sindhu” (Hindi to Hindi Comprehensive digital dictionary); etc. The
Ministry also apprised the Committee about the various schemes of MHA under
Grant No. 51; Border Infrastructure & Management schemes like construction of
border roads, border fencing, border out posts, helipads, foot suspension bridges,
Comprehensive Integrated Border Management System (CIBMS),  implementation
of low cost surveillance system, use of space technology by border guarding

123 
Forces/CRPF; Land Port Management System; launch of Vibrant Village Program; 
fast track immigration facility for pre-vetted international travellers, e-Visa facility 
to nationals of 171 countries; schemes for safety of women - Nirbhaya Fund 
through which operationalisation of 112 Emergency Response Support System 
(ERSS) in 36 States, Anti-Human Trafficking Units (AHTUs), modernization of 
forensic science, safe city projects, etc.; strengthening of Criminal Justice System; 
modernisation of prisons; improved security scenario in Jammu & Kashmir; 
consistent decline in incidents and geographical spread of left wing extremism; 
reduction/removal of ‘disturbed areas’ under AFSPA; Peace Agreements in NE 
States; etc.   
6. The Chairman and Members of the Committee raised queries that inter-alia
included rehabilitation of undertrial prisoners after release from prison without
conviction; concern regarding the housing of undertrials and convicted in the same
jail; reduction in allocation of fund for support to poor prisoners; criminal activities
from jail premises by criminals lodged in jails; low fund allocation for Census
work and the timeline for its completion; using Aadhaar cards, birth and death
records, etc., for census related work.; reasons for low allocation under some sub-
heads for the Financial year 2025-26; provision for relief and rehabilitation for
migrants/repatriates; measures taken to deal with disasters due to global warming;
the criteria for giving Disaster Management Fund to various States; issues related
to Manipur; increased rate of crimes against women; schemes for safety of women;
earthquake preparedness in Delhi and steps taken to assess buildings in Delhi for
earthquake resistance; strengthening of the criminal justice system; low fund
allocation for modernization of Police forces; prevention of mobile phone usage in
prisons; huge fund allocation under sub-head “Research” and whether any
assessment is done on the research papers; update on the implementation of Crime
and Criminal Tracking Network and Systems (CCTNS); provisions of visa and
immigration; steps taken to reduce the number of cases pending for expert opinion
in Central Forensic Labs; influx of illegal immigrants in the country and measures
taken to address it; etc.
7. The Chairman and Members of the Committee gave some suggestions that
inter alia  included provision for separate jail for convicted prisoners and
undertrials; some districts should stay on the list of areas affected by left-wing
extremism instead of being completely removed; separation of law and order from

124 
criminal investigation; joint operation by Central police forces and State Police 
Forces to control the influx of infiltrators and illegal immigrants in the country; 
timely publishing of data by NCRB, Census can be done through electronic mode; 
separate police forces to deal with cyber crimes; possibility of developing airstrips 
in North Eastern Region; allowing online hearings for undertrial prisoners to 
reduce police resources on transportation; to extend budget provision for relief and 
rehabilitation for riot affected people; etc.  
8. Before replying to the queries, the Home Secretary informed the Chairman
that he and the Director, IB would be unable to attend the afternoon session of the
meeting due to unavoidable reasons and sought permission. Thereafter, the Home
Secretary replied to some of the queries raised by the Members. On other queries,
the Ministry was asked to furnish written replies to the Secretariat within two days.
(The witnesses withdrew) 
(The Committee re-assembled at 02:00 p.m. to discuss Demand No. 51 
pertaining to CAPFs and CPOs) 
9. The Chairman welcomed the representatives of the Ministry of Home
Affairs, Directors-General of the Central Armed Police Forces (CAPFs),
representatives of the Central Police Organizations (CPOs) and other officials to
the meeting.
10. The representatives of the Ministry of Home Affairs made a presentation
about the Demands for Grants (2025-26) for the Police (including Central Armed
Police Forces) that inter alia included roles of Border Guarding Forces and Non
Border Guarding Forces and their jurisdiction; their manpower resources;
sanctioned strength and recruitment done; battalions in CAPFs; battalions raised
during last 10 years; overall allocation for CAPFs in Grant No. 51 (Police); Police
Infrastructure; funds provided  for office building, residential buildings;
establishment of Central Armed Police Forces Institute of Medical Sciences
(CAPFIMS); steps taken for welfare measures like higher rate of risk and hardship
allowance for CAPFs personnel posted at J&K, Ladakh and LWE Area,
enhancement of retirement age from 57 to 60 years, higher HRA for persons
posted in J&K and Ladakh, launch of eAwas web Portal for online allotment of
residential accommodation, enhancement of Ex-gratia, online portal ‘Bharat Ke
Veer’ (BKV) developed to facilitate voluntary contributions to the NoKs (next of

125 
kin) of CAPFs personnel killed in action, CAPF personnel receive a 50% GST 
exemption on purchases; etc. important initiatives like decision taken to extend 
medical facilities to force personnel and their family under ‘Ayushman CAPF’, 
bonus marks for NCC cadets in employment, transparent software-based posting 
for CAPF personnel, introduction of millets in their diets, etc.; new initiatives like 
massive plantation by CAPFs; budget allocation to National Investigation Agency 
(NIA); future plans of NCB (Narcotics Control Bureau), BPR&D  and capacity 
building programmes; etc. 
11. After the presentation by the Ministry, the Chairman and Members of the
Committee raised queries on various issues that inter alia  included under
utilization of funds under Capital Expenditure; funds lapsed in border
infrastructure and management scheme; rising attrition rate and suicide rate in
personnel of CAPFs; filling up of vacancies in CAPFs; removal of chapter on
‘agitation’ from BPR&D Report; cadre review in few categories in BSF; reasons
for voluntary retirement or resignations and suicides by CAPFs personnel and steps
taken to address these issues; housing infrastructure and housing satisfaction level
among CAPF personnel; budget curtailment for Indian Cybercrime Coordination
Centre; number of children of the constabulary  which are accommodated in
Kendriya Vidyalayas and Jawahar Navodaya Vidyalayas; provision of appointment
of spouse and children of CAPFs on compassionate grounds in case of unfortunate
death of the personnel; potential of use of Artificial Intelligence to track the
criminals; steps taken to improve the functioning of National Intelligence Grid;
welfare measures undertaken for the CAPF personnel; road construction status in
Nepal border; Status of fencing in Myanmar border; under utilization of funds by
NCB when drugs and narcotics are a major menace in the society; shortage of
manpower in CISF; Ministry’s proposals for effective utilization of  budget
allocation for CPOs; etc.
12. The Chairman and Members also made some suggestions that included
effective and timely utilization of budget allocations, it should not be allowed to be
lapsed; measures to be  taken to improve the housing facilities of the CAPF’s
personnel; timely release of NCRB data and inclusion of chapter on agitation in
BPR&D report; steps should be taken to  provide good quality food to the CAPF
personnel and to increase protein supplement in their diet; dedicated helicopter
service and chopper facilities to NSG,SPG, CRPF, BSF, etc. to reach the place of

126 
disaster; any rule deciding the ex-gratia amount for the death of CAPF personnel, it 
should be removed to avoid misinterpretation of duty-related deaths; enhancement 
of ex-gratia amount; utilisation of funds for welfare activities like establishment of 
canteen, CGHS card facilities;  residential facilities to CISF personnel; use of 
proper earplugs and glasses while shooting practice; due recognition to K-9 (dog 
squad) and increase the number of dog squads; use of modern technology, add a 
column for OBC representation in the forces, alongside SC, ST, and women, to 
clarify their numbers; etc;  
13. The representatives of the Ministry replied to some of the queries raised by
the Members. On other queries, the Ministry was asked to furnish written replies to
the Secretariat within two days.
 (Witnesses withdrew) 
14. A Verbatim record of the proceedings of the meeting was kept.

pertaining to Delhi Police and Union Territories of Delhi (Demand No. 57),

proposals for the year 2025-26 in these UTs.

briefed the Committee about various activities of Delhi Police that inter-alia
included initiatives which have been taken to ease the reporting of crime and
complaints; implementation of new laws and current status of Inter-Operable
Criminal Justice System (ICJS); strengthening of K-9 (dog squad) and Bomb

management system; changes and simplification brought in licenses regulations;
better delivery of services pertaining to passport verifications; initiatives taken for
community outreach; welfare assistance to martyrs; welfare assistance to wards of
Police personnel; technology initiatives like e-forensic software, e-chittha to
automise duty roster, etc.
6. After the completion of the presentation of Delhi Police, the representative
of the Ministry briefed the Committee about the budget transfers given by the
Central Government to the UT of Delhi which included allocation made for
Central assistance, Delhi Disaster Relief Fund, enhancement of compensation
given to 1984 Delhi riot’s victims; and Central assistance for Chandrawal water
treatment plant.

132 
7. Thereafter, he informed the Committee about the budget transfers given by

Puducherry apprised the Committee that the Central assistance becomes part of the
UT’s overall resources and these resources are used to meet the gap in the UT’s
own resources for financing of capital infrastructure and the social welfare
schemes of the Government of Puducherry.

2024-25. The representative of the UT informed that efforts are being made in

education sector, road infrastructure and tourism sector in the UT.
